then
\mathrm{Q}^{\star}=\mathrm{P}^{\star}\circ(\mu,R)^{-1}
where
\mathrm{P}^{\star}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm\varepsilon\mbox{-}mfg}
. Also, for any
\mathrm{P}^{\star}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{0,\;\rm\varepsilon\mbox{-}mfg}
there exist a contract
\mathfrak{C}=(\phi,\xi,\aleph)
and
\mathrm{Q}^{\star}\in{\cal S}^{\star}(\mathfrak{C},\varepsilon)
s.t.
\mathrm{Q}^{\star}=\mathrm{P}^{\star}\circ(\mu,R)^{-1}
.

Proof.

We only prove for
\varepsilon=0,
the case for any
\varepsilon\geq 0
follows immediately. Let
\mathfrak{C}=(\phi,\xi,\aleph)
be a contract and
\mathrm{Q}^{\star}\in{\cal S}^{\star}(\mathfrak{C})
. Let
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\mbox{{\rm MFG}}[\mathfrak{C}]
be s.t.
\mathrm{Q}^{\star}=\mathbb{P}\circ(\mu,R)^{-1}
. We introduce

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\star}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{\alpha},W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}\;\;\mbox{where}\;\;R:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t,\;\Phi(\cdot):=\delta_{\phi(\cdot)}(\mathrm{d}i)\;\mbox{and}\;\mathfrak{Z}(\cdot):=\delta_{\xi(\cdot)}(\mathrm{d}e).

We can easily check that
\mathrm{P}^{\star}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
. By the definition of
(B,R,\mu)
, it is easy to see that (
3.2
) is satisfied for
\mathrm{P}^{\star}
. Now, let
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
be such that
{\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}^{\star}}\big(B,R\big)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\big(B,R\big)
. Notice that, by uniqueness in distribution of (
3.1
), we verify that
{\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}(X^{\prime},W,B,R,\mu)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(Z,W,B,R,\mu)
where
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Z_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}(t,m,e)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+\int_{A}b\left(t,Z_{t},\mu,u\right)\Lambda^{\prime}\left(t,Z_{t},R\right)(\mathrm{d}a)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,Z_{t})\;\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\;\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

We set
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.
\Theta:=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\delta_{Z_{t}}(\mathrm{d}z)\Lambda^{\prime}\left(t,Z_{t},R\right)(\mathrm{d}a)|{\cal G}_{t}\right]\mathrm{d}t\;\mbox{and}\;\;\widetilde{\mu}_{t}:={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(Z_{t}|{\cal G}_{t})
for all
t\in[0,T]
. As in
Remark

3.4
,
B
is a progressively Borel map of
R
under
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
, under
2.1
by
Lemma

3.6
, there exists a sequence
(\lambda^{\prime\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}\subset{\cal A}
s.t. if
Z^{\ell}
is the solution of

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Z^{\ell}_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}(t,m,e)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+b\left(t,Z^{\ell}_{t},\mu,\lambda^{\prime\ell}\left(t,Z^{\ell}_{t},R\right)\right)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,Z^{\ell}_{t})\;\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\;\mathrm{d}B_{t}

then
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J_{A,\overline{\mu}}^{\mathfrak{C}}(\lambda^{\prime\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Theta,\widetilde{\mu},R,\mu)\big]=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big].
Consequently, as we know that
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\mbox{{\rm MFG}}[\mathfrak{C}]
, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}^{\star}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]=J_{A,\overline{\mu}}^{\mathfrak{C}}(\alpha)\geq\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J_{A,\overline{\mu}}^{\mathfrak{C}}(\lambda^{\prime\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big].

Then
\mathrm{P}^{\star}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
.

Let
\mathrm{P}^{\star}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm 0,mfg}
associated to
\Phi=\delta_{\phi(\cdot)}(\mathrm{d}i)
and
\mathfrak{Z}=\delta_{z(\cdot)}(\mathrm{d}e)
.
On the probability space
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
, we consider the process
(B,\overline{m},\overline{e})
s.t.
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(B,\overline{R}\right)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(B,\delta_{\left(\overline{m}_{t},\overline{e}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}^{\star}}(B,R)
where
\overline{R}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{m}_{t},\overline{e}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
. With the Borel map
\alpha
associated to
\mathrm{P}^{\star}(\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm 0,mfg})
, we define
X^{\alpha}
the process verifying:
X^{\alpha}_{0}=\iota
and
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\alpha}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{m}_{t},\overline{e}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+b\left(t,X^{\alpha}_{t},\mu,\alpha\left(t,X^{\alpha}_{t},\mu,\overline{m}_{t}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\alpha}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

Notice that we can find a Borel map
\varphi
s.t.
(\mu_{t\wedge\cdot},\overline{m}_{t})=\varphi(t,\overline{R})

\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e.
We set
\mu_{t}:=\overline{m}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x,A)
,
\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathrm{P}^{\star}
–a.e.,
\overline{\alpha}(t,x,{\rm r}):=\alpha(t,x,\varphi(t,{\rm r}))
and
\mathfrak{C}:=(\phi,z,\overline{e})
. Then, it is easy to verify that
(\overline{\alpha},\overline{m})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
and
\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\mu,R\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}^{\star}\circ(\mu,R)^{-1}
.

∎

3.2
From Nash equilibria to MFG solutions

In this section we give the characterization of the limits of all sequences of approximate Nash equilibria and approximate MFG solutions.

Let us take a control rule
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
satisfying
\Lambda^{\prime}(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t=\delta_{\beta(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t

\mathrm{P}
–a.e. for some progressively Borel measurable map
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{M}\ni(t,x,{\rm r})\mapsto\beta(t,x,{\rm r})\in A
Lipschitz in
(x,{\rm r})
uniformly in
t
. For each
n\geq 1,
we consider
n
progressively Borel measurable functions
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}:=(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n})\in({\cal A}_{n})^{n}
i.e.
\alpha^{i,n}:[0,T]\times({\cal C})^{n}\to A
and we define for a fixed
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\displaystyle\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\beta\left(t,{x}^{i}(t),R^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}[\boldsymbol{x}]\right)

(3.6)

where

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{x}:=(x^{1},\cdots,x^{n}),\;\overline{m}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{\left({x}^{k}(t),\;\alpha^{k,n}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\right)},\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;R^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}[\boldsymbol{x}]:=\delta_{\left(\overline{m}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(t,\boldsymbol{x}),\aleph^{n}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t

for some sequence of contracts
(\mathfrak{C}^{n})_{n\geq 1}=(\phi^{n},\xi^{n},\aleph^{n})_{n\geq 1}
. Let
{\rm G}
be the density
{\rm G}(x):=\frac{1}{1+|x|^{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{1+|x^{\prime}|^{p}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\right)^{-1}
. We recall that the notations we will use below have been introduced in
Section

2.1.1
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
2.1
hold true and a sequence
(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
s.t. for each
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*},

\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}:=(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n})\in({\cal A}_{n})^{n}
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(B,\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],R^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}}[\mathbf{X}^{n}]\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(B,\mu,R\right)^{-1}\;\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mathrm{G}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mathrm{G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\;\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\;\lim_{n\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{n}(\mathbf{X}^{n}),R^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}}[\mathbf{X}^{n}]\right)=\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}(R)(\mathrm{d}r).

Then

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}J_{n,i}^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{i-1,n},\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n},i},\alpha^{i+1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right].

Proof.

The proof is divided into three parts.

\boldsymbol{\underline{\rm Step\;1}}
Given the controls
\boldsymbol{\alpha}:=(\alpha^{1,n},\dots,\alpha^{n,n})\in({\cal A}_{n})^{n},

\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph}:=(X^{1},\dots,X^{n})
satisfies (
2.1
).
For each
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\},
we define

\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{i}:=\left(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{i-1,n},\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i},\alpha^{i+1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n}\right)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\mathbf{Y}^{i}:=(Y^{i,1},\cdots,Y^{i,n}):=\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{i},\aleph}.

Notice that,
Y^{i,i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y^{i,i}_{t}=\overline{b}\big(t,\overline{\mu}^{i,n}_{t},\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i})\big)+b\big(t,Y^{i,i}_{t},\mu^{i,n},\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i})\big)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\big(t,Y^{i,i}_{t}\big)\mathrm{d}W^{i}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\mbox{with}\;Y^{i,i}_{0}=\iota^{i}

with

\mu^{i,n}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{Y^{i,k}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}x)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\overline{\mu}^{i,n}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a):=\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{k\neq i}^{n}\delta_{(Y^{i,k}_{t},\;\alpha^{k,n}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i}))}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)+\delta_{(Y^{i,i}_{t},\;\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i}))}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)\right).

We now introduce

\displaystyle R^{i,n}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{i,n}_{t},\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\;\mbox{and}\;\widetilde{R}^{i,n}:=\delta_{\left(\tilde{\mu}^{i,n}_{t},\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\;\;\mbox{with}\;\;\widetilde{\mu}^{i,n}_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{(Y^{i,k}_{t},\;\alpha^{k,n}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i}))}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a).

We can rewrite

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y^{i,i}_{t}=\langle\overline{b}(t,\cdot,\cdot),R^{i,n}_{t}\rangle+b\left(t,Y^{i,i}_{t},\mu^{i,n},\beta(t,Y^{i,i}_{t},\widetilde{R}^{i,n})\right)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\big(t,Y^{i,i}_{t}\big)\mathrm{d}W^{i}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\mbox{with}\;Y^{i,i}_{0}=\iota^{i}.

Let
(\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
be the sequence defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(Y^{i,i},R^{i,n},\mu^{i,n},\widetilde{R}^{i,n},W^{i},B\right)^{-1}.

Since
\nu\in{\cal P}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
p^{\prime}>p
,
(b,\sigma,\sigma_{0})
is bounded and
{\rm E}\times{\rm I}
is compact, it is straightforward to check that the sequence
(\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
. We recall that for simplicity we have chosen
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
s.t. we can write all our random variables on this space without enlarging the space. Let
\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{\infty}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(Y,R,\mu,\widetilde{R},W,B\right)^{-1}
be the limit of a convergent sub–sequence. It is easy to see that
(W,B)
is an
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
Brownian motion and, by using the continuity of the coefficients and classical weak convergence characterization, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y_{t}=\langle\overline{b}(t,\cdot,\cdot),R_{t}\rangle+b\left(t,Y_{t},\mu,\beta(t,Y_{t},\widetilde{R})\right)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma\big(t,Y_{t}\big)\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\mbox{with}\;{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{0})={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(\iota^{1}).

(3.8)

Notice that by uniqueness of this equation, we can find a measurable map
\Upsilon:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal C}\times{\cal C}\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times\mathbb{M}\times\mathbb{M}\to{\cal C}
s.t.
Y=\Upsilon\left(Y_{0},W,B,\mu,R,\widetilde{R}\right)
. We will show that
{\cal L}\left(Y_{0},W,B,\mu,R,\widetilde{R}\right)=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(X_{0}^{\prime},W,B,\mu,R,R\right)^{-1}
. This will allow us to deduce that
{\cal L}\left(Y,R,\mu,\widetilde{R},W,B\right)=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(X^{\prime},R,\mu,R,W,B\right)^{-1}
.

\boldsymbol{\underline{\rm Step\;2}}

Next, if we set
R^{n}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\varphi}^{n}_{t}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{X})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
, we show that, for
F
and
G
two bounded continuous maps,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})F\left(R^{i,n},\mu^{i,n},\widetilde{R}^{i,n},B\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})F\left(R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],R^{n},B\right)\right].

For this purpose, let us introduce

\displaystyle Z^{i}_{t}:=\exp\bigg\{\int_{0}^{t}\phi^{i}_{r}\mathrm{d}W^{i}_{r}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}|\phi^{i}_{r}|^{2}\mathrm{d}r\bigg\}\;\mbox{for all}\;t\in[0,T],\;\mbox{and}\;\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}^{i}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}:=Z^{i}_{T}

with

\displaystyle\phi^{i}_{t}:=\sigma(t,X^{i}_{t})^{-1}\Big(\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\zeta}^{i,n,\beta}_{t},\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph})\right)

\displaystyle+b\left(t,X^{i}_{t},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(t,\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph})\right)

\displaystyle-\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\varphi}^{n}_{t}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph})\right)-b\left(t,X^{i}_{t},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],\alpha^{i,n}(t,\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph})\right)\Big)

and

\displaystyle\overline{\zeta}^{i,n,\beta}_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{k\neq i}^{n}\delta_{(X^{k}_{t},\;\alpha^{k,n}(t,\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph}))}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)+\delta_{(X^{i}_{t},\;\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(t,\mathbf{X}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},\aleph}))}(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)\right).

By uniqueness in distribution,
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{Q}^{i}}(\mathbf{X}^{\alpha},B)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(\mathbf{Y}^{i},B)
for each
i.
Also, since the difference between
\overline{\mu}^{i,n}_{t}
and
\widetilde{\mu}^{i,n}_{t}
is only
\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}
, it is easy to see that
\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\overline{\mu}^{i,n}_{t},\widetilde{\mu}^{i,n}_{t}\right)=0

\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e. Then, we can notice that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})F\left(R^{i,n},\mu^{i,n},\widetilde{R}^{i,n},B\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})F\left(\widetilde{R}^{i,n},\mu^{i,n},\widetilde{R}^{i,n},B\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[Z^{i}_{T}G\left(X^{i}_{0},W^{i}_{\cdot}-\int_{0}^{\cdot}\phi^{i}_{t}\mathrm{d}t\right)F\left(R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],R^{n},B\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\hat{\mu}^{n}}\left[Z_{T}G\left(X_{0},W_{\cdot}-\int_{0}^{\cdot}\int_{{\cal V}}v\;\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t\right)\right]F\left(R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],R^{n},B\right)\right]

where
(X_{0},Z,W,\Lambda)
is the canonical variable of
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal C}\times{\cal C}\times\mathbb{M}({\cal V})
with
{\cal V}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
an appropirate compact and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}^{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\left(X^{i}_{0},Z^{i},W^{i},\Lambda^{i}\right)}\;\;\mbox{where}\;\;\Lambda^{i}:=\delta_{\phi^{i}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t.

It is easy to see that the sequence
\left(\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\widehat{\mu}^{n},R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],B\right)^{-1}\right)_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
. Let us take a convergent sub–sequence. For simplification, we use the same notation for the sequence and the sub–sequence. So, we are considering
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\widehat{\mu}^{n},R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],B\right)^{-1}=\mathbb{P}\circ(\widehat{\mu},R,\mu,B)^{-1}
. By observing that
\mathrm{d}Z^{i}_{t}=Z^{i}_{t}\phi^{i}_{t}\mathrm{d}W^{i}_{t}
and
Z^{i}_{0}=1
for each
i
, then by using similar techniques as in (
3.8
) (see also
[
17
, Proof of Proposition 4.17.]
), we can verify that
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.
\widehat{\mu}=\widehat{\mu}\circ(X_{0},Z,W,\Lambda)^{-1}
,
\widehat{\mu}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Z_{t}=Z_{t}\int_{{\cal V}}v\;M(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}t)\mbox{ and }W_{t}=M({\cal V}\times[0,t])\mbox{ for all }t\in[0,T]

where
Z_{0}=1
and
M
is a
\widehat{\mu}
–martingale measure with quadratic variation
\Lambda
(see
[
19
]
for an overview on martingale measure). We define
\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}^{\hat{\mu}}}{\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}}:=Z_{T}
. By Girsanov Theorem, we can see that under
\mathbb{P}^{\hat{\mu}}
,
W_{\cdot}-\int_{0}^{\cdot}\int_{{\cal V}}v\;\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t
is a Brownian motion. Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[G(Y_{0},W)F\left(R,\mu,\widetilde{R},B\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})F\left({R}^{i,n},\mu^{i,n},\widetilde{R}^{i,n},B\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}^{\hat{\mu}}}\left[G\left(X_{0},W_{\cdot}-\int_{0}^{\cdot}\int_{{\cal V}}v\;\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t\right)\right]F(R,\mu,R,B)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{1}_{0},W)\right]\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[F(R,\mu,R,B)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})F\left({R}^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}],{R}^{n},B\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[G(X_{0}^{\prime},W)F\left(R,\mu,R,B\right)\right]

(3.9)

where we used the law of large number with the i.i.d sequence
(W^{i},\iota^{i})_{i\geq 1}
. This result remains true for any convergent sub–sequence of
\left(\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\widehat{\mu}^{n},R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],B\right)^{-1}\right)_{n\geq 1}
and any bounded maps
(G,F)
. It is enough to conclude that
{\cal L}\left(Y_{0},W,B,\mu,R,\widetilde{R}\right)=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(X_{0}^{\prime},W,B,\mu,R,R\right)^{-1}
, so

\lim_{n\to\infty}\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{n}={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(Y,R,\mu,\widetilde{R},W,B\right)=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(X^{\prime},R,\mu,R,W,B\right)^{-1}.

\boldsymbol{\underline{\rm Step\;3}}

Let
(\mathrm{Q}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
be defined by
\mathrm{Q}^{n}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\eta^{n}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\eta^{n}\right)^{-1}
where
\eta^{n}_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{Y^{i,i}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}x)
. Since we know that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mathrm{G}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mathrm{G}(x)\mathrm{d}x
, by
Proposition

A.2
, the sequence
(\mathrm{Q}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
, and any limit point
\mathrm{Q}=\mathbb{P}\circ(\kappa,\eta)^{-1}
satisfies:
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.
\kappa=\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\eta_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)
. Notice that,

\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\eta_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\cal L}(Y^{i,i}_{T})=\mathrm{P}\circ(X_{T}^{\prime})^{-1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[g\left(Y^{i,i}_{T},\phi^{n}(Y^{i,i}_{T})\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{{\rm I}\times\mathbb{R}}g\left(x,i\right)\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\eta^{n}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{{\rm I}\times\mathbb{R}}g\left(x,i\right)\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\eta_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{{\rm I}}g\left(X_{T}^{\prime},i\right)\Phi(X_{T}^{\prime})(\mathrm{d}i)\right].

Also, since
\mu_{T}
is a Borel map of
R
, by the same techniques used in (
3.2
) through change of probability, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\overline{g}\left(\mu^{i,n},\xi^{n}(\mathbf{Y}^{i})\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[Z^{i}_{T}\overline{g}\left(\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],\xi^{n}(\mathbf{X})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\overline{g}\left(\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],\xi^{n}(\mathbf{X})\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[\int_{{\rm E}}\overline{g}\left(\mu,e\right)\mathfrak{Z}(R)(\mathrm{d}e)\right].

By combining all the results, we find

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}J_{n,i}^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}\big(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{i-1,n},\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i},\alpha^{i+1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n}\big)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\overline{L}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{i,n}_{t},\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i})\right)+L\left(t,Y^{i,i}_{t},\mu^{i,n},\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha},i}(t,\mathbf{Y}^{i})\right)\;\mathrm{d}t+\overline{g}\left(\mu^{i,n},\xi^{n}(\mathbf{Y}^{i})\right)+g\big(Y^{i,i}_{T},\phi^{n}(Y^{i,i}_{T})\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right].

∎

For each
n\geq 1
, we consider the controls
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}:=(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n})\in({\cal A}_{n})^{n}
. Let us introduce

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{i},W^{i},B,R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]\right)^{-1}\;\;

\mbox{where}\;R^{n}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\varphi}^{n}_{t}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{X}_{t})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t.
We also consider the sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\geq 1}
satisfying
\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0.

Proposition 3.9
.

Under
2.1
, the sequence
\left(\mathrm{P}^{n}\right)_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
with
p^{\prime}>p
, and any limit point is a control rule in the sense of
Definition

3.1
.
Moreover, if for each
n\geq 1
,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}
is an
\varepsilon_{n}
–Nash equilibrium then any limit point
\mathrm{P}
of a convergent sub–sequence
\left(\mathrm{P}^{n_{k}}\right)_{k\geq 1}
is a MFG solution i.e. belongs to
\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
associated to
(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})
which verifies:
\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{n_{k}}(\mathbf{X}),\;R^{n_{k}}\right)=\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(R\right)(\mathrm{d}r)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\varphi^{n_{k}}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n_{k}}],\;\delta_{\phi^{n_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\varphi^{n_{k}}_{T}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n_{k}}](\mathrm{d}x)\right)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu,\;\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}.

Proof.

We set
\Lambda^{i}:=\delta_{\alpha^{i,n}(t,\mathbf{X})}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t
and we define the sequence
(\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\Lambda^{i},X^{i},W^{i},B,R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]\right)^{-1}.

Since the initial distribution
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(X^{1}_{0})\in{\cal W}_{p^{\prime}}
, under
2.1
, it is straightforward to check that
(\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
with
p^{\prime}>p
. Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}
be the limit of a convergent sub–sequence of
(\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
. For simplification, we use the same notation for the sequence and its sub–sequence.
Notice that, we can rewrite the dynamics of
X^{i}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{i}_{t}=\langle\overline{b}\left(t,\cdot,\cdot\right),R^{n}_{t}\rangle+\int_{A}b\left(t,X^{i}_{t},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],a\right)\Lambda^{i}_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{i}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{i}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

By using some classical martingale problem (see for instance
[
17
]
), we can check that
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\Lambda,X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
where
W
and
B
are independent Brownian motions and
X
satisfies:
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.,
X_{0}=\iota
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\langle\overline{b}\left(t,\cdot,\cdot\right),R_{t}\rangle+\int_{A}b\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

Let
F
,
V
and
G
be two bounded continuous functions

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[V\left(X_{0}\right)F\left(W\right)G\left(B,R,\mu\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[V\left(X^{i}_{0}\right)F\left(W^{i}\right)G\left(B,R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[V\left(X_{0}\right)\right]\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[F\left(W\right)\right]\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[G\left(B,R,\mu\right)\right]

where we use the fact that
(X^{i}_{0},W^{i})_{i\geq 1}
is an i.i.d. sequence combined with the law of large number. This is true for any maps
F
,
V
and
G
, we can deduce that
(B,R,\mu)
,
W
and
X_{0}
are independent. By the definition of
R^{n}
and
\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]
, it is easy to check that
\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
–a.e.
R_{t}\left(\{(m,e):\;m(\mathrm{d}x,A)=\mu_{t}\}\right)=1
(see also the proof of
[
14
, Proposition 4.4]
). We can then see
\mu_{t}
as a Borel map of
R_{t}
. Next, we verify that
\mu_{t}={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(X_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}\right)
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. for all
t\in[0,T]
where
{\cal G}_{t}:=\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot},R_{t\wedge\cdot}\}
. For this purpose, let
t\in[0,T]
and,
f
and
G
be two bounded continuous maps, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[f(X_{t})G\left(B,R\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[f(X^{i}_{t})G\left(B,R^{n}\right)\right]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\langle f,\varphi^{n}_{t}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]\rangle G\left(B,R^{n}\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\langle f,\mu_{t}\rangle G\left(B,R\right)\right].

This is true for any
(f,G)
. This is enough to conclude that
\mu_{t}={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(X_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}\right)
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. for all
t\in[0,T]
. Now, for any bounded continuous maps
(h,\varphi,G)
, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}h(t)\varphi(X_{t})b\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t\;G\left(B,R\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}h(t)\varphi(X^{i}_{t})b\left(t,X^{i}_{t},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],a\right)\Lambda^{i}_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t\;G\left(B,R^{n}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}h(t)\varphi(x)b\left(t,x,\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],a\right)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)R^{n}_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})\mathrm{d}t\;G\left(B,R^{n}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}h(t)\varphi(x)b\left(t,x,\mu,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})\mathrm{d}t\;G\left(B,R\right)\right].

This is true for any
(h,\varphi,G)
. We deduce that
\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{A}\left(\varphi b\right)\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)|{\cal G}_{T}\right]=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}\left(\varphi b\right)\left(t,x,\mu,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E}).

By similar arguments, we get
\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{A}L\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)|{\cal G}_{T}\right]=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}L\left(t,x,\mu,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})
,
\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e. We set
\Lambda(t,x,{\rm r})(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t:=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}m(x)(\mathrm{d}a){\rm r}(t)(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})\mathrm{d}t
where for
m\in{\cal P}_{A}
,
\mathbb{R}\ni x\mapsto m(x)\in{\cal P}(A)
is a Borel map satisfying
m(x)(\mathrm{d}a)m(\mathrm{d}x,A)=m
. The previous result combined with
Lemma

A.1
allows to verify that
\mu_{t}={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(S_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}\right)
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. where

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}S_{t}=\langle\overline{b}\left(t,\cdot,\cdot\right),R_{t}\rangle+\int_{A}b\left(t,S_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda(t,S_{t},R)(\mathrm{d}a)\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,S_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

By combining all the results, we find that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathrm{P}^{n}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
. Consequently,
\mathrm{P}=\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}\circ\left(X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
is a control rule (see
Remark

3.2
) . This is true for any convergent sub–sequence.

We now prove the second part of the Proposition i.e.
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
. Notice that the sequences
\left(\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)_{n\geq 1}
and
\left(\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\xi^{n}(\mathbf{X}^{n}),R^{n}\right)^{-1}\right)_{n\geq 1}
are relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
. Let
\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x
and
\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(R\right)(\mathrm{d}r)
be the limits of a sub–sequence. We choose the sub–sequence
(n_{k})_{k\geq 1}
s.t.

\displaystyle(\mathrm{P}^{n_{k}})_{k\geq 1},\;\left(\delta_{\phi^{n_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)_{k\geq 1}\mbox{ and }\left(\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\xi^{n_{k}}(\mathbf{X}^{n_{k}}),R^{n_{k}}\right)^{-1}\right)_{k\geq 1}

are convergent. Again, we use the same notation for the sequence and the sub–sequence. It is straightforward that
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(R)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}(R)
and by using
Proposition

A.2
, we find that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\varphi^{n}_{T}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}](\mathrm{d}x),\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],R^{n}\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\mu,R\right)^{-1}.

We now check the optimality property. Let
\mathrm{Q}
be a control rule s.t.
{\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}(B,R)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{Q}}(B,R)
. Thanks to
Lemma

3.6
, we can assume that
\mathrm{d}\mathrm{Q}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
–a.e.
\Lambda^{\prime}(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)(\mathrm{d}a)=\delta_{\beta(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)}(\mathrm{d}a)
where
\beta
is a Lipschitz map in
(x,{\rm r})
uniformly in
t
.
Next, we can use
Lemma

3.8
and find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{Q}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}J_{n,i}^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}\big(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{i-1,n},\beta^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n},i},\alpha^{i+1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}J_{n,i}^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}\left(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}\right)+\varepsilon_{n}=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big].

We can therefore conclude the proof of the Proposition.
∎

We provide here a result similar to
Lemma

3.8
for approximate MFG solutions. We take a control rule
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
satisfying
\Lambda^{\prime}(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t=\delta_{\beta(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t

\mathrm{P}
–a.e. for some progressively Borel map
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{M}\ni(t,x,{\rm r})\mapsto\beta(t,x,{\rm r})\in A
Lipschitz in
(x,{\rm r})
uniformly in
t
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
2.1
hold true. We consider a sequence of contracts
\left(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}=(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})\right)_{\ell\geq 1}
and a sequence
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t. for each
\ell\geq 1,

(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell}]
,

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(B,\mu^{\ell},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(B,\mu,R\right)^{-1}\;\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}

and

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\delta_{\phi^{\ell}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mathrm{G}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mathrm{G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\;\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\;\lim_{\ell\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{\ell}(\overline{\mu}^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell}),\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)=\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(R\right)(\mathrm{d}r).

Then, there exists a sequence
(\beta^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}\subset{\cal A}
s.t.

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}(\beta^{\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right].

Proof.

Recall
X^{\prime}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\prime}_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}(t,m,e)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+b(t,X^{\prime}_{t},\mu_{t},\beta(t,X^{\prime}_{t},R))\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\prime}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\mbox{with}\;{\cal L}(X^{\prime}_{0})=\nu.

On the space
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
, we introduce
X^{\prime\ell}
the solution of:
{\cal L}(X^{\prime\ell}_{0})=\nu
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\prime\ell}_{t}=\overline{b}(t,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t})+b(t,X^{\prime\ell}_{t},\mu^{\ell},\beta(t,X^{\prime\ell}_{t},R^{\ell}))\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\prime\ell}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\mbox{with}\;\;R^{\ell}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t.

Since
\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(B,\mu^{\ell},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(B,\mu,R\right)^{-1}
and
\left(B,\mu^{\ell},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)
,
X_{0}
and
W
are independent for each
\ell\geq 1
, by using martingale problem combined with similar arguments to
Lemma

3.8
, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{\prime\ell},W,B,\mu^{\ell},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\xi^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}\circ\left(X^{\prime},W,B,\mu,R\right)^{-1}.

Again, by the similar techniques used in
Lemma

3.8
(without change of probability), we deduce that
\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}(\beta^{\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right]
.
∎

With the sequence
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
given in

3.10
i.e. for each
\ell\geq 1
,
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell}]

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\ell}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{\alpha^{\ell}},W,B,R^{\ell},\mu^{\ell}\right)^{-1}\;\;

\mbox{where}\;\;R^{\ell}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
and

is a sequence of non–negative numbers satisfying
\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\ell}=0.

Proposition 3.11
.

Under
2.1
, the sequence
\left(\mathrm{P}^{\ell}\right)_{\ell\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
with

, and any limit point
\mathrm{P}

and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{\ell_{k}},\;\delta_{\phi^{n_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mu^{\ell_{k}}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu,\;\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}.

Proof.

The proof is quite similar to the proof of
Proposition

3.9
. For any
\ell\geq 1
, we can rewrite
X^{\alpha^{\ell}}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\alpha^{\ell}}_{t}=\langle\overline{b}\left(t,\cdot,\cdot\right),R^{\ell}_{t}\rangle+\int_{A}b\left(t,X^{\alpha^{\ell}}_{t},\mu^{\ell},a\right)\Lambda^{\ell}_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\alpha^{\ell}}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\;\mbox{with}\;\;\Lambda^{\ell}:=\delta_{\alpha^{\ell}(t,X^{\alpha^{\ell}}_{t},R^{\ell})}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t.

We define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{\ell}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\Lambda^{\ell},X^{\alpha^{\ell}},W,B,R^{\ell},\mu^{\ell}\right)^{-1}.

Usual techniques allow to say that the sequence
(\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
is relatively compact
{\cal W}_{p}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\Lambda,X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
be the limit of a convergent sub–sequence. Similar to the previous proof, we use the same notation for the sequence and the sub–sequence. By standard techniques, the probability
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\Lambda,X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
is s.t.
(W,B)
is an
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
–valued Brownian motion,
X
verifies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\langle\overline{b}\left(t,\cdot,\cdot\right),R_{t}\rangle+\int_{A}b\left(t,X_{t},\mu_{t},a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

We set
\mathbb{G}=({\cal G}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}:=\left(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot},R_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
. By using the similar approach used in the proof of
Proposition

3.9
, we easily check that
\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e.,
R_{t}\left(\{(m,e):\;\mu_{t}=m(\mathrm{d}x,A)\}\right)=1
,
\mu_{t}={\cal L}(X_{t}|{\cal G}_{T})
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{A}\left(\varphi b\right)\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)|{\cal G}_{T}\right]=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}\left(\varphi b\right)\left(t,x,\mu,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})\;\;\mbox{for any }\varphi\in C_{c},

and,
\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{A}L\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)|{\cal G}_{T}\right]=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}L\left(t,x,\mu,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})
,
\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e. We can find a Borel map
K
verifying
K(t,x,{\rm r})=m(x)(\mathrm{d}u){\rm r}(t)(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})
where
(m(x))_{x\in\mathbb{R}}
satisfies

. It follows that (see
Lemma

A.1
)
\mu_{t}={\cal L}(S_{t}|{\cal G}_{T})
where
{\cal L}(S_{0})=\nu
and

We deduce that
\mathrm{P}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
(see
Remark

3.2
). The probability
\mathrm{P}
is in fact in
\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}.
We consider the unique sub–sequence
(\ell_{k})_{k\geq 1}
s.t.
(\mathrm{P}^{\ell_{k}})_{k\geq 1}
,
\left(\delta_{\phi^{\ell_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\right)_{k\geq 1}
and

are convergent sequences. Let
\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x
and
\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(R\right)(\mathrm{d}r)
be the limit of

and

. By

, we deduce that
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
associated to

, with

satisfying the convergence mentioned.
∎

3.3
From MFG solutions to approximate Nash equilibria

We now deal in this section with the construction of approximate MFG solutions and approximate Nash equilibria from MFG solutions.

On the probability space
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
, we consider the processes
(X,W,B,R,\mu)
s.t.
(W,B)
is an
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
–Brownian motion,
R
is an
{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}
–valued
\mathbb{H}
–predictable process,

,
R_{t}\left(\{(m,e):\;\;m(\mathrm{d}x,A)=\mu_{t}\}\right)=1

\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
–a.e. and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}\left(t,m,e\right)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{A}b\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)m(X_{t})(\mathrm{d}a)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

where

Lemma 3.12
.

There exists a sequence

2.1
, it is straightforward to check that

We can find a sequence of an

–valued

(\widetilde{m}^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}

\mathbb{P}
–a.e. By using
[
14

, there exists a sequence of

–valued
\mathbb{F}
–predictable processes
(\alpha^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t. if

\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y^{\ell}_{t}=\overline{h}\left(t,B_{t},\overline{m}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)+h\left(t,B_{t},Y^{\ell}_{t},\widetilde{\mu}^{\ell},\alpha^{\ell}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+v(t,B_{t},Y^{\ell}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t},\;\overline{m}^{\ell}_{t}:={\cal L}(Y^{\ell}_{t},\alpha^{\ell}_{t}|{\cal G}_{t})\;\mbox{and}\;\widetilde{\mu}^{\ell}_{t}:={\cal L}(Y^{\ell}_{t}|{\cal G}_{t})

we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\overline{m}^{\ell}_{t},\widetilde{m}^{\ell}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{t}^{\ell},\widetilde{\mu}_{t}\right)\right]=0.

It is easy to check that
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\overline{m}^{\ell}_{t}[B_{t}],\widetilde{m}^{\ell}_{t}[B_{t}]\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{t}^{\ell}[B_{t}],\widetilde{\mu}_{t}[B_{t}]\right)\right]=0
. Notice that,
\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\delta_{m^{\prime}[B_{t}]}(\mathrm{d}m)\Theta_{t}(\mathrm{d}m^{\prime},\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t=R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{t}[B_{t}]=\mu_{t}
and if we set
\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t}:=\overline{m}^{\ell}_{t}[B_{t}]
,
\mu_{t}^{\ell}:=\widetilde{\mu}^{\ell}_{t}[B_{t}]
,
X^{\ell}_{\cdot}:=Y^{\ell}_{\cdot}+\sigma_{0}B_{\cdot}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\ell}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X^{\ell}_{t},\mu^{\ell}_{t},\alpha^{\ell}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\ell}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}

and, up to a sub–sequence,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.,
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\delta_{(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t})}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t=R
and
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mu^{\ell}=\mu
.
This is enough to deduce the proof of the first part of the Proposition. The second part is concluded by (an easy adaptation of)
Proposition

A.2
.

∎

Let
\alpha
be an
A
–valued
\mathbb{F}
–predictable process and
\aleph
be an
{\rm E}
–valued
\mathbb{F}
–predictable process. We consider
X
satisfying:
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(X_{0})=\nu
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X_{t},\mu_{t},\alpha_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\overline{\mu}_{t}={\cal L}(X_{t},\alpha_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}),\;\mu_{t}=\left(X_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}\right),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.},

where
{\cal G}_{t}:=\sigma\{R_{t\wedge\cdot},B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}
with
R:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t.
The variables
(R,B)
,
X_{0}
and
W
are independent.

Let
(\varepsilon_{k})_{k\geq 1}
be a sequence of positive numbers verifying
\lim_{k\to\infty}\varepsilon_{k}=0
. And, we set
W^{k}_{\cdot}:=W_{\cdot\vee\varepsilon_{k}}-W_{\varepsilon_{k}}
and
B^{k}_{\cdot}:=B_{\cdot\vee\varepsilon_{k}}-B_{\varepsilon_{k}}
.

Lemma 3.13
.

There exist a sequence of piece–wise constant
A
–valued
\mathbb{F}
–predictable processes
(\alpha^{k})_{k\geq 1}
and a sequence of piece–wise constant
{\rm E}
–valued
\mathbb{G}
–predictable processes
(\aleph^{k})_{k\geq 1}
s.t.

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|X^{k}_{t}-X_{t}\right|^{p}+\int_{0}^{T}\rho_{A}\left(\alpha_{t},\alpha^{k}_{t}\right)^{p}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\rho_{{\rm E}}\left(\aleph_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t}\right)^{p}\mathrm{d}t\right]=0

where
X^{k}
is satisfying:
X^{k}_{t}=X_{0}
for
t\in[0,\varepsilon_{k}]
and
\mbox{for }t\in[\varepsilon_{k},T],\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{k}_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X^{k}_{t},\mu^{k}_{t},\alpha^{k}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{k}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\;\overline{\mu}^{k}_{t}={\cal L}(X^{k}_{t},\alpha^{k}_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}),\;\mu^{k}_{t}={\cal L}\left(X^{k}_{t}|{\cal G}_{T}\right),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

(3.10)

Proof.

This result comes from an application of
[
17
, Lemma 4.3]
. To justify the measurability of the processes
(\alpha^{k})_{k\geq 1}
and
(\aleph^{k})_{k\geq 1}
, it is enough to see that these processes are constructed by considering first a sub–division
0=t^{k}_{0}<t^{k}_{1}:=\varepsilon^{k}<\cdots<t^{k}_{k}=T
verifying
\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{0\leq l\leq k-1}|t^{k}_{l+1}-t^{k}_{l}|
. And, second by taking

\displaystyle\alpha^{k}_{t}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\int_{\left([t]^{k}-\varepsilon_{k}\right)\vee 0}^{[t]^{k}}\alpha_{s}\mathrm{d}s\;\mbox{and}\;\aleph^{k}_{t}:=\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{k}}\int_{\left([t]^{k}-\varepsilon_{k}\right)\vee 0}^{[t]^{k}}\aleph_{s}\mathrm{d}s\;\mbox{where}\;[t]^{k}:=t^{k}_{l}\;\mbox{for}\;t\in[t^{k}_{l},t^{k}_{l+1})\;\mbox{with}\;[T]^{k}:=T.

∎

Lemma 3.14
.

Let us stay in the context of the previous
Lemma

3.13
with
k\geq 1
.

There exist an
A
–valued
\left(\sigma\{X_{0},W_{t\wedge\cdot},B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process
\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}
, and an
{\rm E}
–valued
\left(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
s.t.

\displaystyle{\cal L}\left(X^{k},W^{k},B^{k},\Lambda^{k},R^{k}\right)={\cal L}\left(\widetilde{X}^{k},W^{k},B^{k},\widetilde{\Lambda}^{k},\widetilde{R}^{k}\right)

(3.11)

where
X^{k}
is given in
Equation

3.10
with
\Lambda^{k}:=\delta_{\alpha^{k}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t
,
R^{k}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{k}_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
,
\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t}=X_{0}
for
t\in[0,\varepsilon_{k}]
and
\mbox{for }t\in[\varepsilon_{k},T],\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t},\zeta^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{k}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\;\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t}={\cal L}(\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}_{t}|B),\;\zeta^{k}_{t}={\cal L}(\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t}|B),\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

with
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{k}:=\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t
and
\widetilde{R}^{k}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\tilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
.

Proof.

It is essentially an application of
[
17
, Lemma 4.4]
, we just need to check the measurability of the different process
\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}
and
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
. Indeed, if we apply
[
17
, Lemma 4.4]
, we find that the processes
\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}
and
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
given in the statement of our Lemma has to be
\left(\sigma\{A^{k},X_{0},W_{t\wedge\cdot},B^{k}_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable with
A^{k}
an
[0,1]
–valued uniform random variable independent of
(X_{0},W,B^{k})
. But, since for each
k
,
(\aleph^{k},\overline{\mu}^{k})
,
X_{0}
and
W
are independent, to get the equality in distribution given in
Equation

3.11
(similar to
[
17
, Equation 4.7 of Lemma 4.4]
), we see that the construction of
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
can indeed be done without needing the variables
(X_{0},W)
. Therefore
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
can be taken
\left(\sigma\{A^{k},B^{k}_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable. In addition, we know that
\varepsilon_{k}>0
and
B
is an
\mathbb{F}
–Brownian motion, so its increments are independent and we can take
A^{k}=F(B_{\varepsilon_{k}\wedge\cdot})
for an appropriate Borel map
F
. Consequently,
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
is
\left(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable and
\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}
is
\left(\sigma\{X_{0},W_{t\wedge\cdot},B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable.

∎

With the processes
\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}
and
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
given in
Lemma

3.14
. We introduce the process
S^{k}
satisfying:
S^{k}_{0}=X_{0}
, and for
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}S^{k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\eta}^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,S^{k}_{t},\eta^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,S^{k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\overline{\eta}^{k}_{t}={\cal L}(S^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\alpha}^{k}_{t}|B),\;\eta^{k}_{t}={\cal L}\left(S^{k}_{t}|B\right),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

We also define
\Psi^{k}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\eta}^{k}_{t},\tilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
,
D^{k}:=\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t
and
\Lambda:=\delta_{\alpha_{t}}(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t
.

Lemma 3.15
.

We have
\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|S^{k}_{t}-\widetilde{X}^{k}_{t}|^{p}\right]=0
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}\left(S^{k},W,B,D^{k},\Psi^{k}\right)={\cal L}\left(X,W,B,\Lambda,R\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}

Proof.

The main difference between
S^{k}
and
\widetilde{X}^{k}
is the shifting of the Brownian motions
W
and
B
. By seeing that
W_{\varepsilon^{k}\wedge\cdot}
and
B_{\varepsilon^{k}\wedge\cdot}
converge to
0
when
k\to\infty
, this is enough to deduce the
\mathbb{L}^{p}
–convergence by using some classical arguments since the map
(b,\sigma)
is Lipschitz in
(x,\pi,m)
. For the Wasserstein convergence, it is a combination of this
\mathbb{L}^{p}
–convergence combined with the equality in distribution in
Lemma

3.14
and the
\mathbb{L}^{p}
–convergence in
Lemma

3.13
.
∎

Now, let
\alpha
be an
A
–valued
\left(\sigma\{X_{0},W_{t\wedge\cdot},B_{t\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process and
\aleph
be an
{\rm E}
–valued
\left(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process. We consider
X
satisfying:
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(X_{0})=\nu
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X_{t},\mu_{t},\alpha_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\overline{\mu}_{t}={\cal L}(X_{t},\alpha_{t}|B),\;\mu_{t}={\cal L}\left(X_{t}|B\right),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Lemma 3.16
.

There exists a sequence of progressively Borel measurable maps
(\aleph^{j},\alpha^{j})_{j\geq 1}
s.t. for each
j\geq 1
,
[0,T]\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\ni(t,\pi)\mapsto\aleph^{j}(t,\pi_{t\wedge\cdot})\in{\rm E}
and
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\ni(t,x,\pi)\mapsto\alpha^{j}(t,x,\pi_{t\wedge\cdot})\in A
are Lipschitz maps in
(x,\pi)
uniformly in
t
and, if we define
X^{j}
the solution of:
\aleph^{j}_{t}:=\aleph^{j}(t,\mu^{j})
,
\alpha^{j}_{t}=\alpha^{j}(t,X^{j}_{t},\mu^{j})
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{j}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{j}_{t},\aleph^{j}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X^{j}_{t},\mu^{j}_{t},\alpha^{j}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{j}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\overline{\mu}^{j}_{t}={\cal L}(X^{j}_{t},\alpha^{j}_{t}|B_{t\wedge\cdot}),\;\mu^{j}_{t}=(X^{j}_{t}|B_{t\wedge\cdot}),\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.},

then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{j\to\infty}\left(\mu^{j},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{j}_{t},\aleph^{j}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)=\left(\mu,\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)\;\;\mbox{in }{\cal W}_{p},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Proof.

\boldsymbol{{\rm Step\;1}}
Since
\alpha
is an
\left(\sigma\{X_{0},W_{t\wedge\cdot},B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process and
\aleph
is an
\left(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process, by abusing the notations, we find progressively measurable Borel maps
\alpha:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times{\cal C}\times{\cal C}\to A
and
\aleph:[0,T]\times{\cal C}\to{\rm E}
s.t.
\alpha_{t}=\alpha(t,X_{0},W,B)
and
\aleph_{t}=\aleph(t,B)

\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e. Let
{\rm b}\in{\cal C}
. We set
\alpha^{{\rm b}}_{t}:=\alpha(t,X_{0},W,{\rm b})
and
\aleph^{{\rm b}}_{t}:=\aleph(t,{\rm b})
. Let
X^{{\rm b}}
be the solution of

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(X^{{\rm b}}_{t}-\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t)\right)=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t},\aleph^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X^{{\rm b}}_{t},\mu^{{\rm b}}_{t},\alpha^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{{\rm b}}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

with
\;\overline{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}={\cal L}(X^{{\rm b}}_{t},\alpha^{{\rm b}}_{t}),\;\mu^{{\rm b}}_{t}={\cal L}(X^{{\rm b}}_{t}).
By uniqueness in distribution, we check that
{\cal L}(X,W|B={\rm b})={\cal L}(X^{{\rm b}},W)
. Similarly to the previous proof of
Lemma

3.12
, we use some shifting. We introduce the following quantities: for each
(m,\pi)
,
m[{\rm b}(t)](\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}a):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{x+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t)}(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)
and
\pi(t)[{\rm b}](\mathrm{d}y):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{x+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t)}(\mathrm{d}y)\pi(t)(\mathrm{d}x)
. We set
\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}:=\overline{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}[-{\rm b}(t)]
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}:=\mu^{{\rm b}}_{t}[-{\rm b}]
and
Y^{{\rm b}}_{t}=X^{{\rm b}}_{t}-\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. The processes
\left(\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t},\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t},Y^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y^{{\rm b}}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}[{\rm b}(t)],\aleph^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,Y^{{\rm b}}_{t}+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t),\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}[{\rm b}],\alpha^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,Y^{{\rm b}}_{t}+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t))\mathrm{d}W_{t}\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

with
\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(\mathrm{d}y,A)=\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}
. We define the maps

\displaystyle\overline{h}\left(t,{\rm b},m,e\right):=\overline{b}\left(t,m[{\rm b}(t)],e\right),\;\;h\left(t,{\rm b},y,\pi,a\right):=b\left(t,y+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t),\pi[{\rm b}],a\right)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;v(t,{\rm b},y):=\sigma(t,y+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t)).

Under
2.1
, it is straightforward to check that

[0,T]\times{\cal C}\times{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times A\ni(t,{\rm b},m,e,y,\pi,a)\mapsto\left(\overline{h}\left(t,{\rm b},m,e\right),h\left(t,{\rm b},y,\pi,a\right),v(t,{\rm b},y)\right)

is continuous in
({\rm b},m,e,y,\pi,a)
for each
t
and Lipschitz in
(m,y,\pi)
uniformly in
(t,{\rm b},e,a)
.
We can rewrite
Y^{{\rm b}}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y^{{\rm b}}_{t}=\overline{h}\left(t,{\rm b},\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t},\aleph^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)+h\left(t,{\rm b},Y^{{\rm b}}_{t},\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}},\alpha^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+v(t,{\rm b},Y^{{\rm b}}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

By applying Itô formula and taking the expectation, we find: for all
\varphi

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\langle\varphi,\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}\rangle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}\varphi^{\prime}(y)\;\;\overline{h}(t,{\rm b},\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t},\aleph^{{\rm b}}_{t})+h\left(t,{\rm b},y,\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}},a\right)\;\;\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\langle\varphi^{\prime\prime}(\cdot)v^{2}(t,{\rm b},\cdot),\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}\rangle\mathrm{d}t.

Let us consider the Borel map
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(x)\in{\cal P}(A)
verifying
\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}=\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(x)(\mathrm{d}a)\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(\mathrm{d}x)
. Notice that, we can choose
\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(x)
s.t. the map
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal C}\ni(t,x,{\rm b})\mapsto\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(x)\in{\cal P}(A)
is Borel measurable . Since
{\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x
is non–atomic, there exists a sequence of progressively Borel maps
\left(\widetilde{\alpha}^{l}\right)_{l\geq 1}
s.t.
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal C}\ni(t,x,{\rm b})\mapsto\widetilde{\alpha}^{l}(t,x,{\rm b})\in A
and

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\delta_{\tilde{\alpha}^{l}\left(t,x,{\rm b}\right)}(\mathrm{d}a){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}(x)(\mathrm{d}a){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}

(3.12)

\;\mbox{where }{\rm G}\mbox{ is a continuous density}

{\rm G}(x):=\frac{1}{1+|x|^{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{p}}\mathrm{d}y\right)^{-1}.

Now, let
Y^{{\rm b},l}
be the solution of

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t}=\overline{h}\left(t,{\rm b},\overline{m}^{{\rm b},l}_{t},\aleph^{{\rm b}}_{t}\right)+h\left(t,{\rm b},Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t},\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b},l}_{t},\widetilde{\alpha}^{l}(t,Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t},{\rm b})\right)\mathrm{d}t+v(t,{\rm b},Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

with
\overline{m}^{{\rm b},l}_{t}:={\cal L}\left(Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t},\widetilde{\alpha}^{l}(t,Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t},{\rm b})\right)
and
\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b},l}_{t}:={\cal L}(Y^{{\rm b},l}_{t})
. Combined (
3.12
) and (an easy extension of)
[
15
, Proposition A.4.]
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b},l},\delta_{\overline{m}^{{\rm b},l}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t\right)=\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}},\delta_{\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}.

We can also check that
\lim_{l\to\infty}\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b},l}[{\rm b}],\delta_{\overline{m}^{{\rm b},l}_{t}[{\rm b}]}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t\right)=\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{{\rm b}}[{\rm b}],\delta_{\overline{m}^{{\rm b}}_{t}[{\rm b}]}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t\right)
. Now, let us consider
X^{l}
the process satisfying

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{l}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{l}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X^{l}_{t},\mu^{l}_{t},\alpha^{l}\left(t,X^{l}_{t},B\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{l}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

with
\overline{\mu}^{l}_{t}={\cal L}\left(X^{l}_{t},\alpha^{l}\left(t,X^{l}_{t},B\right)|B_{t\wedge\cdot}\right),\;\mu^{l}_{t}=\left(X^{l}_{t}|B_{t\wedge\cdot}\right)
and
\alpha^{l}(t,x,{\rm b})=\widetilde{\alpha}^{l}(t,x-\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(t),{\rm b})
. By uniqueness, we can check that
{\cal L}(Y^{{\rm b},l}_{\cdot},W_{\cdot})={\cal L}(X^{l}_{\cdot}-\sigma_{0}B_{\cdot},W_{\cdot}|B={\rm b})
. We can deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{l\to\infty}\left(\mu^{l},\delta_{\overline{\mu}^{l}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t\right)=\left(\mu,\delta_{\overline{\mu}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

By using
[
15
, Proposition A.7.]
, up to another approximation, we can consider that the map
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal C}\ni(t,x,b)\mapsto\left(\alpha^{l}(t,x,b),\aleph(t,b)\right)\in A\times{\rm E}
are Lipschitz in
(x,b)
uniformly in
t
.

\boldsymbol{{\rm Step\;2}}
Let
l\geq 1
. We know that
\sigma_{0}
is invertible. Then, we can use
[
15
, Proposition A.10.]
and find a sequence of Borel progressively measurable maps

\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\ni(t,x,\pi)\mapsto\left(\alpha^{l,j}(t,x,\pi),\aleph^{j}(t,\pi),\phi^{j}(t,\pi)\right)\in A\times{\rm E}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)_{j\geq 1}

Lipschitz in
(x,\pi)
uniformly in
t
s.t.
\left(\alpha^{l,j}(t,x,\pi),\aleph^{j}(t,\pi)\right)=\left(\alpha^{l}(t,x,\phi^{j}(t,\pi)),\aleph(t,\phi^{j}(t,\pi))\right)
and if we let
X^{l,j}
be the process satisfying

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{l,j}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{l,j}_{t},\aleph^{l}(t,\mu^{l,j}_{t})\right)+b\left(t,X^{l,j}_{t},\mu^{l,j}_{t},\alpha^{l,j}\left(t,X^{l,j}_{t},\mu^{l,j}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{l,j}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

with
\overline{\mu}^{l,j}_{t}={\cal L}\left(X^{l,j}_{t},\alpha^{l,j}\left(t,X^{l,j}_{t},\mu^{l,j}\right)|B_{t\wedge\cdot}\right),\;\mu^{l,j}_{t}=\left(X^{l,j}_{t}|B_{t\wedge\cdot}\right)
, we have
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. for all
t\in[0,T]
and each
l,j\geq 1
,
B_{t}=\phi^{k}(t,\mu^{l,j})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\|\mu^{l,j}_{t}-\mu^{l}_{t}\|_{\rm TV}\mathrm{d}t\right]=0\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\lim_{j\to\infty}\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{l,j}_{t},\aleph^{l}(t,\mu^{l,j}_{t})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t=\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{l}_{t},\aleph^{l}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p},\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

where
\|\|_{\rm TV}
is the total variation distance. By combining the previous 2 steps, we can deduce the proof of the Lemma.

∎

Let us consider the random variables
(\mu,R)
and the filtration
\mathbb{G}
given in the (preamble of )
Lemma

3.12
. By combining all the previous results, we are able to prove that:

Proposition 3.17
.

There exist:

•

a sequence
(\mu^{k},\overline{\mu}^{k},\aleph^{k})_{k\geq 1}
s.t.
\mu^{k}
is an
{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
–valued
\mathbb{G}
–adapted continuous process,
\overline{\mu}^{k}
is an
{\cal P}_{A}
–valued
\mathbb{G}
–predictable process and
\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}
is an
{\rm E}
–valued
\mathbb{G}
–predictable process;

•

a sequence
(\zeta^{k},\overline{\zeta}^{k},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
s.t.
\zeta^{k}
is an
{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
–valued
(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\})_{t\in[0,T]}
–adapted continuous process,
\overline{\zeta}^{k}
is an
{\cal P}_{A}
–valued
(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\})_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process and
\aleph^{k}
is an
{\rm E}
–valued
(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\})_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable process;

•

a sequence of Borel progressively measurable maps
(\aleph^{j,k},\alpha^{j,k,\ell})
s.t.
[0,T]\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\ni(t,\pi)\to\aleph^{j,k}(t,\pi_{t\wedge\cdot})\in{\rm E}
and
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\ni(t,x,\pi)\to\alpha^{j,k}(t,x,\pi_{t\wedge\cdot})\in A
are Lipschitz maps in
(x,\pi)
uniformly in
t
and, if we define
X^{j,k}:=X
the solution of:
\aleph^{j,k}_{t}:=\aleph^{j,k}(t,\mu^{j,k})
,
\alpha^{j,k}_{t}=\alpha^{j,k}(t,X^{j,k}_{t},\mu^{j,k})
,
\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t}={\cal L}(X_{t},\alpha^{j,k}_{t}|B_{t\wedge\cdot}),\;\mu^{j,k}_{t}=(X_{t}|B_{t\wedge\cdot})
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\aleph^{j,k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,X_{t},\mu^{j,k}_{t},\alpha^{j,k}_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.},

all these sequences satisfy: first,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\left(\mu^{j,k},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\aleph^{j,k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right),\left(\zeta^{k},\delta_{\left(\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\tilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)\right)=0,\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e. },

second, for each
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\zeta^{k},\delta_{\left(\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\tilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t,B^{k},W^{k},X_{0}\right)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{k},\delta_{\left(\mu^{k}_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t,B^{k},W^{k},X_{0}\right)

and finally

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal W}_{p}\left(\left(\mu^{k},\delta_{\left(\mu^{k}_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right),\left(\mu,R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)\right)=0,\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e. }

All the previous results have been a preparation to provide the following Proposition which states the approximation of MFG solution by a sequence of approximate strong MFG solutions.

Proposition 3.18
.

Let
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm\varepsilon\mbox{-}mfg}
associated to
(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})
. There exist a sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
and a sequence
(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell},\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t. for each
\ell\geq 1
,
(\alpha^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})\in{\cal C}_{s}
,
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in{\rm MFG}_{S}\left[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell}\right]
where
\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}:=(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell}\varepsilon_{\ell}\in[0,\varepsilon]\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\lim_{\ell\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{\ell},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu,R\right)\;\;\mbox{in }{\cal W}_{p}.

Besides,

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}\left(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu\right)\big]\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J_{\mathrm{P}}^{\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{R,\mu}_{\mathrm{P}}(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})\big].

Proof.

For simplification, we can assume that
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X,W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
with the random variables
(X,W,B,R,\mu)
defined on
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
. Therefore, we will only use the probability space
(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P})
.

\boldsymbol{{\rm Step\;1}}

First, by combining the approximation of measurable function by continuous functions in
[
8
, Proposition C.1]
and the approximation of continuous function by Lipschitz functions in
[
9
, Theorem 6.4.1]
, there exists a sequence of Lipschitz functions
\left(\xi^{\ell}:\mathbb{M}\to{\rm E}\right)_{\ell\geq 1}
and
\left(\phi^{\ell}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\rm I}\right)_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t.

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\delta_{\xi^{\ell}({\rm r})}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(R)(\mathrm{d}r)=\mathfrak{Z}({\rm r})(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(R)(\mathrm{d}r)\;\mbox{and}\;\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\delta_{\phi^{\ell}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x=\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x.

We are then using
Proposition

3.17
, we consider the sequence
(\alpha^{j,k},\overline{\mu}^{j,k},\aleph^{j,k})_{j,k\geq 1}
given in the Proposition and we have

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{j,k},\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\aleph^{j,k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu,R\right)\;\;\mbox{in }{\cal W}_{p}.

We can check that: in
{\cal W}_{p}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{\ell}(\mu^{j,k}),\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\;\aleph^{j,k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi,R\right)

and with the help of
Proposition

A.2
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}

\displaystyle{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{j,k},\;\delta_{\phi^{\ell}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mu^{j,k}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\;\aleph^{j,k}_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu,\;\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;R\right).

By setting
\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}:=(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{j,k})
, this allows us to verify that

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}J_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}(\alpha^{j,k})=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}\left(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu\right)\big]\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}J_{\mathrm{P}}^{\alpha^{j,k},\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell})=\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{R,\mu}_{\mathrm{P}}(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})\big].

To complete the proof, we need to check that
(\alpha^{j,k},\overline{\mu}^{j,k})\in{\rm MFG}_{S}[\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell},\varepsilon_{j,k,\ell}]
for some sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{j,k,\ell})_{j,k,\ell\geq 1}
verifying
\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}\varepsilon_{j,k,\ell}\in[0,\varepsilon]
.

\boldsymbol{{\rm Step\;2}}
Let us recall that
(\varepsilon^{k},W^{k},B^{k})_{k\geq 1}
is given in
Lemma

3.13
and
(\mu^{k},\overline{\mu}^{k},\aleph^{k},\zeta^{k},\overline{\zeta}^{k},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
is given in
Proposition

3.17
. We set
\widetilde{R}^{k}:=\delta_{(\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\tilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t})}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
and
R^{k}:=\delta_{(\overline{\mu}^{k}_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t})}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
. By using the equality in distribution given in
Proposition

3.17
, it is straightforward that for each
\beta\in{\cal A}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}:={\cal L}\left(\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k},W^{k},B^{k},\widetilde{R}^{k},\zeta^{k}\right)={\cal L}\left(\widehat{X}^{\beta,k},W^{k},B^{k},R^{k},\mu^{k}\right)=:\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}

with
(\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k},\widehat{X}^{\beta,k})
satisfying:
\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k}_{t}=\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}_{t}=X_{0}
for
t\in[0,\varepsilon^{k}]
, for
t\in[\varepsilon^{k},T]
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k}_{t},\zeta^{k}_{t},\beta\left(t,\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k}_{t},\widetilde{R}^{k}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{k}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.},

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{k}_{t},\aleph^{k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}_{t},\mu^{k}_{t},\beta\left(t,\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}_{t},R^{k}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{k}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Consequently, if we consider
\Phi^{\ell}(\cdot):=\delta_{\phi^{\ell}(\cdot)}(\mathrm{d}i)
and
\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}(\cdot):=\delta_{\xi^{\ell}(\cdot)}(\mathrm{d}e)
, we find

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]=\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big].

(3.13)

Notice that, the canonical variable
\Gamma^{\prime}
is not well defined in the previous equality since
\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}
do not belong to
\overline{{\cal P}}
. Indeed, the processes
\widetilde{X}^{\beta,k}
and
\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}
are frozen on
[0,\varepsilon^{k}]
. We just froze
\Gamma^{\prime}
on
[0,\varepsilon^{k}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A
and abuse the notation by considering that the distributions belong to
\overline{{\cal P}}
.

By using the almost surely convergence in
Proposition

3.17
and the regularity of the coefficients, we can check that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\left|\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]-\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]\right|=0

where

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}:={\cal L}\left(X^{\beta,k},W,B,R,\mu\right)

with
X^{\beta,k}
verifying

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\beta,k}_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}\left(t,m,e\right)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+b\left(t,X^{\beta,k}_{t},\mu,\beta\left(t,\widehat{X}^{\beta,k}_{t},R^{k}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\beta,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Since
(R^{k}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is an
\mathbb{G}
–predictable process for each
k
, let us point out the fact that
(R^{k}_{t\wedge\cdot},B_{t\wedge\cdot})
is a Borel map of
R_{t\wedge\cdot}
(see also
Remark

3.4
). Then, a projection argument like in
Lemma

A.1
allows us to find a progressively Borel map
\Lambda^{\beta,k}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{M}\to{\cal P}(A)
s.t.
{\cal L}(X^{\beta,k}_{t}|R_{t\wedge\cdot})={\cal L}(X^{\beta,k}_{t}|R)={\cal L}(Z^{\beta,k}_{t}|R)
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. with

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Z^{\beta,k}_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}\left(t,m,e\right)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+\int_{A}b\left(t,Z^{\beta,k}_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda^{\beta,k}\left(t,Z^{\beta}_{t},R\right)(\mathrm{d}a)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,Z^{\beta,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

This leads to say that
\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
with
\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}\circ(B,R)^{-1}=\mathrm{P}\circ(B,R)^{-1}
. As a result, we obtain that:,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\bigg(\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\langle\overline{L}\left(t,\cdot,\cdot\right),R_{t}\rangle+\langle L\left(t,\cdot,\mu,\cdot\right),\Gamma_{t}^{\prime}\rangle\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\rm I}}\overline{g}(x,i)\Phi^{\ell}(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}x)\right]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{{\rm E}}g(\mu,e)\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}(R)(\mathrm{d}e)\right]\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{k\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]~\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]+\varepsilon.

(3.14)

Next, for any
\beta\in{\cal A}
, given
M^{j,k}:=\delta_{(\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\aleph^{j,k}_{t})}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
, we consider
S^{\beta,j,k}
satisfying:
S^{\beta,j,k}=X_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}S^{\beta,j,k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}_{t},\aleph^{j,k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,S^{\beta,j,k}_{t},\mu^{j,k},\beta\left(t,S^{\beta,j,k}_{t},M^{j,k}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,S^{\beta,j,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Let us now considering
\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}
verifying:
\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}=X_{0}
for
t\in[0,\varepsilon_{k}]
, and for
t\in[\varepsilon_{k},T]
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)+b\left(t,\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t},\zeta^{k},\beta\left(t,S^{\beta,j,k}_{t},M^{j,k}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{k}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

By using the almost surely convergence in
Proposition

3.17
, it is easy to verify that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}-S^{\beta,j,k}_{t}|^{p}\right]=0.

Notice that
(M^{j,k}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
is
\left(\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
–predictable. Therefore, we can find
\widetilde{\beta}^{j,k}
s.t.
\beta\left(t,S^{\beta,j,k}_{t},M^{j,k}\right)=\widetilde{\beta}^{j,k}\left(t,S^{\beta,j,k}_{t},B\right)
. Also, by similar techniques mentioned
Remark

3.4
, we can see that
B^{k}_{t\wedge\cdot}
is a Borel map of
\widetilde{R}^{k}_{t\wedge\cdot}
. Thanks to some projection arguments (see
Lemma

A.1
), we can find a progressively Borel map
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\beta,j,k}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{M}\to{\cal P}(A)
s.t. we get that
{\cal L}(\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}|B^{k}_{t\wedge\cdot})={\cal L}(\widetilde{S}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}|B^{k})={\cal L}(\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}|\widetilde{R}^{k})
where:
\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}=X_{0}
for
t\in[0,\varepsilon_{k}]
, and for
t\in[\varepsilon_{k},T],

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k}_{t}=\overline{b}\left(t,\overline{\zeta}^{k}_{t},\widetilde{\aleph}^{k}_{t}\right)+\int_{A}b\left(t,\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k}_{t},\zeta^{k},a\right)\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\beta,j,k}\left(t,\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k}_{t},\widetilde{R}^{k}\right)(\mathrm{d}a)\;\;\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{k}_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Consequently, by combining the previous result, if we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,j,k}:={\cal L}\left(\widetilde{Z}^{\beta,j,k},W,B,\widetilde{R}^{k},\zeta^{k}\right),

by
Equation

3.13
and
Section

3.3
we find that,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\beta)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,j,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{Q}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}\mathbb{E}^{\widehat{\mathrm{P}}^{\beta,k}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi^{\ell},\mathfrak{Z}^{\ell}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]+\varepsilon.

\boldsymbol{{\rm Step\;3}}

For each
j,k,\ell\geq 1
, we set

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{j,k,\ell}:=\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\beta)-J^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\alpha^{j,k}).

By definition, we have
\varepsilon_{j,k,\ell}\geq 0
for each
j,k,\ell\geq 1
. Also, by using the previous parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}\varepsilon_{j,k,\ell}

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\beta)-\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\alpha^{j,k})

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\ell\to\infty}\limsup_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{j\to\infty}\sup_{\beta\in{\cal A}}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{j,k,\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{j,k}}(\beta)-\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]\leq\varepsilon.

This is enough to conclude the result.

∎

Let
\varepsilon\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{C}=(\phi,\xi,\aleph)
be a contract and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in{\rm MFG}_{S}[\mathfrak{C},\varepsilon]
. We define the contract
\mathfrak{C}^{n}:=(\phi,\xi^{n},\aleph^{n})
and the maps
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}:=\left(\alpha^{1,n},\cdots,\alpha^{n,n}\right)\in({\cal A}_{n})^{n}
by

\displaystyle\xi^{n}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\xi\left(R[\pi^{n}]\right),\;\;\aleph^{n}(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\aleph(t,\pi^{n})\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\alpha^{i,n}(t,\boldsymbol{x}):=\alpha(t,x^{i}(t),\pi^{n}),\;1\leq i\leq n,\;\mbox{with}\;\boldsymbol{x}:=\left(x^{1},\cdots,x^{n}\right)\in({\cal C})^{n},

\;\pi^{n}(t):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{x^{i}(t)}
,
\overline{\pi}^{n}(t):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(x^{i}(t),\alpha^{i,n}(t,\boldsymbol{x}))}
and
R[\pi^{n}]:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\pi}(t),\aleph^{n}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
. We now give the approximation by approximate Nash equilibria.

Proposition 3.19
.

There exists a sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\geq 1}
s.t.
\limsup_{n}\varepsilon_{n}\in[0,\varepsilon]
and for each
n\geq 1
,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}
belongs to
{\rm NE}[\mathfrak{C}^{n},\varepsilon_{n}]
,
