\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],R[\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]]\right)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu,\delta_{\left(\overline{\mu}_{t},\aleph_{t}\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t\right)\;\;\mbox{in }{\cal W}_{p}

and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}_{n,i}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n})=J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}({\alpha})\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\lim_{n\to\infty}J_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}}}(\mathfrak{C}^{n})=J_{\mathrm{P}}^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}(\mathfrak{C}).

Proof.

This is just an application of
[
15
, Proposition 3.15]
. Indeed, given the map
(\xi,\aleph)
, let us observe that if we define the map
(\widehat{B},\widehat{L},\widehat{L}_{\mathrm{P}}):[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{M}\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times A\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{g}_{\mathrm{P}}\right):{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\to\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle(\widehat{B},\widehat{L},\widehat{L}_{\mathrm{P}})(t,x,m,\pi,a):=\left(\overline{b},\overline{L},\overline{L}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)(t,m,\aleph(t,\pi))+\left(b,L,{\rm 0}\right)(t,x,\pi,a),\;\;\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{g}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)(\pi):=\left(\overline{g},\overline{g}_{\mathrm{P}}\right)(\pi,\xi(R[\pi]))

the maps
(\widehat{B},\widehat{L},\widehat{L}_{\mathrm{P}},\sigma,\sigma_{0},\widehat{g},\widehat{g}_{\mathrm{P}})
satisfy the assumptions of
[
15
, Proposition 3.15]
. Therefore, we just apply
[
15
, Proposition 3.15]
to conclude the proof of the Proposition.
∎

3.4
Existence of MFG

This part is dedicated to showing that the set
{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
is non–empty for any contract
\mathfrak{C}
. Let us first considering the case without common noise. We introduce the following quantities: for each
t\in[0,T]
,
(m,\pi)
and
c\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
m[c](\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}a):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{x+\sigma_{0}c}(\mathrm{d}y)m(\mathrm{d}x,\mathrm{d}a)
and
\pi(t)[c](\mathrm{d}y):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\delta_{x+\sigma_{0}c}(\mathrm{d}y)\pi(t)(\mathrm{d}x)
. We define the maps

\displaystyle\overline{h}\left(t,c,m,e\right):=\overline{b}\left(t,m[c],e\right),\;\;\left(h,F\right)\left(t,c,y,\pi,a\right):=\left(b,L\right)\left(t,y+\sigma_{0}c,\pi[c],a\right)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;v(t,c,y):=\sigma(t,y+\sigma_{0}c).

Notice that for each
c
, the maps
(\overline{h},\overline{F})
,
(h,F)
and
v
are continuous in
(c,m,e,y,\pi,a)
for each
t
and Lipschitz in
(m,y,\pi)
uniformly in
(t,c,e,a)
and
v(\cdot,c,\cdot)
. Also,
\inf_{(t,c,y)}vv^{\top}>0.

We say
({\rm q,\pi})
belongs to
\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm no}
if
\mathrm{d}t
–a.e.
{\rm q}(t)\left(\{m:\;m(\mathrm{d}y,A)=\pi(t)\}\right)=1
and
\pi(0)=\nu
.
Given
({\rm b},e)
, for
({\rm q,\pi})\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm no}
, we will say
(\widetilde{\rm q},\widetilde{\rm\pi})\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[{\rm q},\pi]
if
(\widetilde{\rm q},\widetilde{\rm\pi})\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm no}
and, for all
(t,f)
,

\displaystyle 0=\langle f,\widetilde{\rm\pi}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle-\bigg[\langle f,\nu\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{\prime}(y)\overline{h}\left(r,{\rm b}(r),m,e(r)\right)\widetilde{\rm\pi}(r)(\mathrm{d}y)\;{\rm q}(r)(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle~~~~+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}f^{\prime}(y)h\left(r,{\rm b}(r),y,\pi,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}a)\;\widetilde{\rm q}(r)(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f^{\prime\prime}(y)v(r,{\rm b}(r),y)^{2}\widetilde{\rm\pi}(r)(\mathrm{d}y)\mathrm{d}r\bigg]

\displaystyle=:N^{b,e}_{\pi,{\rm q}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}](t,f).

We also set

\displaystyle\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}]:=

\displaystyle\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A}F\left(t,{\rm b}(t),y,\pi,a\right)m(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}a)\widetilde{\rm q}(t)(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}}g\left(y+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(T),\phi\left(y+\sigma_{0}{\rm b}(T)\right)\right)\widetilde{\rm\pi}(T)(\mathrm{d}x).

We define the set

\displaystyle{\cal R}^{\phi,e,{\rm b}}_{\pi,{\rm q}}:=\arg\max_{(\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q})\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[{\rm q},\pi]}\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}]

Proposition 3.20
.

Under
2.1
, for any
({\rm b},e)
and
\phi
, there exists
(q,\pi)
belonging to
{\cal R}^{\phi,e,{\rm b}}_{\pi,{\rm q}}
.

Proof.

This proof is essentially a simple extension of
[
16
, Section 4]
. Let
(\phi^{k})_{k\geq 1}
be a regularization of
\phi
i.e. for each
k\geq 1
,
\phi^{k}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
\lim_{k\to\infty}\phi^{k}=\phi
a.e. Then, for each
k\geq 1
, by
[
16
, Theorem 4.6]
, we can find
(\pi^{k},q^{k})\in{\cal R}^{\phi^{k},e,{\rm b}}_{\pi,{\rm q}}
. Since the coefficients are bounded and
\nu\in{\cal P}_{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
p^{\prime}>p
, we know that the sequence
(\pi^{k},q^{k})_{k\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
(see also
[
16
, Lemma 4.2]
). Let
(\pi,q)
be the limit of a specific convergent sub–sequence
(\pi^{k_{j}},q^{k_{j}})_{j\geq 1}
. Also, we can check that
(\pi,q)\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[q,\pi]
, and by using similar techniques to
Proposition

A.2
, we have

\lim_{j\to\infty}\overline{J}_{q^{k_{j}},\pi^{k_{j}}}^{\phi^{k_{j}},{\rm b}}[\pi^{k_{j}},q^{k_{j}}]=\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\pi,q].

For any
(\widetilde{\rm q},\widetilde{\rm\pi})\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[{\rm q},\pi]
, by techniques borrowed from
[
16
, Proposition 4.5.]
, there exists
(\widetilde{\rm\pi}^{j},\widetilde{\rm q}^{j})_{j\geq 1}
verifying:
(\widetilde{\rm\pi}^{j},\widetilde{\rm q}^{j})\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[{\rm q}^{k_{j}},\pi^{k_{j}}]
for each
j\geq 1
,
\lim_{j\to\infty}\left(\widetilde{\rm\pi}^{j},\widetilde{\rm q}^{j}\right)=\left(\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}\right)
in
{\cal W}_{p}
and
\lim_{j\to\infty}\overline{J}_{q^{k_{j}},\pi^{k_{j}}}^{\phi^{k_{j}},{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi}^{j},\widetilde{\rm q}^{j}]=\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}]
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\pi,q]=\lim_{j\to\infty}\overline{J}_{q^{k_{j}},\pi^{k_{j}}}^{\phi^{k_{j}},{\rm b}}[\pi^{k_{j}},q^{k_{j}}]\geq\lim_{j\to\infty}\overline{J}_{q^{k_{j}},\pi^{k_{j}}}^{\phi^{k_{j}},{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi}^{j},\widetilde{\rm q}^{j}]=\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}].

∎

We recall that
{\rm L}^{p}([0,T];{\rm E})
is the
{\rm L}^{p}
–space of Borel measurable map
e:[0,T]\to{\rm E}
s.t.
\int_{0}^{T}\rho_{{\rm E}}(e(t),e_{0})^{p}\mathrm{d}t<\infty
for some
e_{0}\in{\rm E}
. With the distance
{\cal W}(e,e^{\prime})^{p}:=\int_{0}^{T}\rho_{{\rm E}}(e(t),e^{\prime}(t))^{p}\mathrm{d}t,

\left({\rm L}^{p}([0,T];{\rm E}),{\cal W}\right)
is a Polish space.

Proposition 3.21
.

There exists a universally measurable map
\Psi:{\cal C}\times{\rm L}^{p}([0,T];{\rm E})\to{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times\mathbb{M}
s.t. for each
({\rm b},e,z)
,
\Psi({\rm b},e)\in{\cal R}^{\phi,e,{\rm b}}_{\Psi({\rm b},e)}
.

Proof.

This proof is largely inspired by the proof of
[
18
, Lemma 4.7.]
. We will apply a selection measurable theorem. We introduce

\displaystyle K^{1}_{t}[f,\widetilde{v},\widetilde{h}]:=\left\{\left({\rm b},e,\pi,{\rm q},\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}\right):\;N^{{\rm b},e}_{\pi,{\rm q}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}](t,f)=0,\;\;\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}}\widetilde{h}(t)\left(\langle\widetilde{v},m(\mathrm{d}x,A)\rangle-\langle\widetilde{v},\widetilde{\rm\pi}(t)\rangle\right)\widetilde{\rm q}(t)(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t=0\right\}

and

\displaystyle K^{2}[v,h]:=\left\{\left({\rm b},e,\pi,{\rm q},\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}\right):\;\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}}h(t)\left(\langle v,m(\mathrm{d}x,A)\rangle-\langle v,\pi(t)\rangle\right){\rm q}(t)(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t=0\right\}.

Let us observe that for any
(t,f,v,h,\widetilde{v},\widetilde{h})
, the sets
K^{1}_{t}[f,\widetilde{v},\widetilde{h}]
and
K^{2}[v,h]
are Borel sets. We get that

\displaystyle\left\{\left({\rm b},e,\pi,q,\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}\right):\;\;(\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q})\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[{\rm q},\pi],\;(\pi,{\rm q})\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm no}\right\}=\bigcap_{(t,f,v,h,\widetilde{v},\widetilde{h})\in\mathbb{X}}K^{1}_{t}[f,\widetilde{v},\widetilde{h}]\cap K^{2}[v,h]

where
\mathbb{X}
is a suitable countable dense set of
[0,T]\times C^{\infty}_{c}\times C^{\infty}_{c}\times C([0,T])\times C^{\infty}_{c}\times C([0,T])
. Therefore, since the map
{\cal C}\times{\rm L}^{p}([0,T];{\rm E})\times({\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times\mathbb{M})^{2}\ni({\rm b},e,\pi,q,\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q})\mapsto\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}]\in\mathbb{R}
is Borel measurable, by
[
20
, Proposition 2.21.]
, the map
{\cal C}\times{\rm L}^{p}([0,T];{\rm E})\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times\mathbb{M}\ni({\rm b},e,\pi,q)\mapsto\overline{V}^{\phi}[{\rm b},e,\pi,q]\in\mathbb{R}
is universally measurable where

\displaystyle\overline{V}^{\phi}[{\rm b},e,\pi,q]:=\sup_{(\tilde{\pi},\tilde{q})\in\overline{{\cal P}}^{{\rm b},e}_{\rm no}[{\rm q},\pi]}\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\widetilde{\rm\pi},\widetilde{\rm q}].

If we define

\displaystyle K^{2}_{t}[f,v,h]:=\left\{\left({\rm b},e,\pi,q\right):\;N^{{\rm b},e}_{\pi,{\rm q}}[\pi,q](t,f)=0,\;\;\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}}h(t)\left(\langle v,m(\mathrm{d}x,A)\rangle-\langle v,\pi(t)\rangle\right){\rm q}(t)(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t=0\right\},

we find that the set of mean field game solutions
{\cal S}^{\star}\subset{\cal C}\times{\rm L}^{p}([0,T;{\rm E}])\times{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times\mathbb{M}
is a universally measurable set because

\displaystyle{\cal S}^{\star}:=\left\{({\rm b},e,\pi,q):\;(\pi,q)\in{\cal R}^{\phi,e,{\rm b}}_{\pi,{\rm q}}\right\}=\left\{\left({\rm b},e,\pi,q\right):\;\overline{V}^{\phi}[{\rm b},e,\pi,q]\leq\overline{J}_{q,\pi}^{\phi,{\rm b}}[\pi,q]\right\}\bigcap\left(\cap_{(t,f,v,h)\in\mathbb{Z}}K^{2}_{t}[f,v,h]\right)

where
\mathbb{Z}
is a suitable countable dense set of
[0,T]\times C^{\infty}_{c}\times C^{\infty}_{c}\times C([0,T])
.
Consequently, by applying
[
20
, Theorem 2.20.]
, there exists a universally measurable map
\Psi:{\cal C}\times{\rm L}^{p}([0,T];{\rm E})\to{\cal C}_{{\cal W}}\times\mathbb{M}
verifying
\Psi({\rm b},e)\in{\cal R}^{\phi,e,{\rm b}}_{\Psi({\rm b},e)}
for each
({\rm b},e)
.
∎

Proposition 3.22
.

Under
2.1
and
2.11
, for any contract
\mathfrak{C}=(\phi,\xi,\aleph)
, the set
{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
is non–empty.

Proof.

We know that
\aleph
is an
{\rm E}
–valued
\mathbb{F}
–predictable process s.t.
(\aleph,B)
,
\iota
and
W
are independent. With the map
\Psi
given in
Proposition

3.21
, we define
\widetilde{R}:=\widetilde{Q}_{t}(\mathrm{d}m)\delta_{\aleph_{t}}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
with

\displaystyle(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\eta}):=\Psi\left(B,\aleph\right)\;\;\mbox{and we set}\;\left({\cal G}_{t}:=\sigma\{B_{t\wedge\cdot},\widetilde{R}_{t\wedge\cdot},\widetilde{\eta}_{t\wedge\cdot}\}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}.

We know that
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.
N_{\tilde{\eta},\tilde{Q}}[\widetilde{\eta},\widetilde{Q}](t,f)=0
for all
(t,f)
. Then, we can see that
{\cal L}(Y_{t}|B,\aleph)={\cal L}(Y_{t}|{\cal G}_{t})=\widetilde{\eta}_{t}
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. where
Y
satisfies
{\cal L}(Y_{0})=\nu
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Y_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\overline{h}\left(t,B_{t},m,\aleph_{t}\right)\widetilde{Q}_{t}(\mathrm{d}m)+\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{A}h\left(t,B_{t},Y_{t},\widetilde{\eta},a\right)m^{Y_{t}}(\mathrm{d}a)\widetilde{Q}_{t}(\mathrm{d}m)\;\;\;\mathrm{d}t+v(t,B_{t},Y_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

If we define
X_{\cdot}:=Y_{\cdot}+\sigma_{0}B_{\cdot}
. The process
X
follows the dynamics

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}\left(t,m,e\right)K_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\int_{A}b\left(t,X_{t},\mu,a\right)m^{X_{t}}_{t}(\mathrm{d}a)K_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

where
K_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t:=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}\delta_{m^{\prime}[B_{t}]}(\mathrm{d}m)\widetilde{Q}_{t}(\mathrm{d}m^{\prime})\delta_{\aleph_{t}}(\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
and
\mu_{t}:=\widetilde{\eta}_{t}[B_{t}]
for each
t\in[0,T]
.
We can find a Borel map
\Lambda:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{M}\to{\cal P}(A)
s.t.
\Lambda(t,X_{t},K)(\mathrm{d}a):=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}}m^{X_{t}}(\mathrm{d}a)K_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,{\rm E})
. Notice that
K_{t}\left(\{(m,e):\;m(\mathrm{d}x,A)=\mu_{t}\}\right)=1
,
\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
–a.e.

Let
\overline{\mathrm{P}}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X,W,B,K,\mu\right)^{-1}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
. Let us check the optimality condition of a MFG solution. Let
\mathrm{P}\in\overline{{\cal P}}
s.t.
{\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}(B,R)={\cal L}^{\overline{\mathrm{P}}}(B,R)
. We know that we can write
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{\prime},W,B,R,\mu\right)^{-1}
with

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{\prime}_{t}=\int_{{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{b}\left(t,m,e\right)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)+\int_{A}b\left(t,X^{\prime}_{t},\mu,a\right)\Lambda^{\prime}(t,X^{\prime}_{t},R)(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}t+\sigma(t,X^{\prime}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}+\sigma_{0}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

We define
\widetilde{\mu}_{t}:={\cal L}(X^{\prime}_{t}-\sigma_{0}B_{t}|{\cal G}_{t})
and
\widetilde{H}:=\delta_{\overline{v}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}m)\mathrm{d}t
where
\overline{v}_{t}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\delta_{X^{\prime}_{t}-\sigma_{0}B_{t}}(\mathrm{d}y)\Lambda^{\prime}(t,X_{t}^{\prime},R)(\mathrm{d}a)|{\cal G}_{t}\right]
. We then find that,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.
\omega\in\Omega
,
(\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{H})\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm no}
,
N_{\tilde{\eta},\tilde{Q}}[\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{H}](t,f)=0
for all
(t,f)
, and

\displaystyle\overline{J}_{\tilde{Q},\tilde{\eta}}^{\phi,\aleph,B}[\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{H}]\leq\overline{J}_{\tilde{Q},\tilde{\eta}}^{\phi,\aleph,B}[\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\eta}].

We recall that
\xi:\mathbb{M}\to{\rm E}
is a Borel map, consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[{\rm J}_{A}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}(\Lambda^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[\overline{J}_{\tilde{Q},\tilde{\eta}}^{\phi,\aleph,B}[\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{H}]+\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{L}\left(t,m,e\right)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t+\overline{g}\left(\mu,\xi(R)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{P}}\left[\overline{J}_{\tilde{Q},\tilde{\eta}}^{\phi,\aleph,B}[\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{\eta}]+\int_{[0,T]\times{\cal P}_{A}\times{\rm E}}\overline{L}\left(t,m,e\right)R_{t}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t+\overline{g}\left(\mu,\xi(R)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\overline{\mathrm{P}}}\left[{\rm J}_{A}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}(\Lambda^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right]

We can deduce that
\overline{\mathrm{P}}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
. And, with
Proposition

3.5
, we get
\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}\in\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}^{W}
s.t.
\mathbb{E}^{\overline{\mathrm{P}}}\left[{\rm J}_{A}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right]\leq\mathbb{E}^{\widetilde{\mathrm{P}}}\left[{\rm J}_{A}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\right]
.

∎

3.5
Proof of the main results

3.5.1
Proof of
Theorem

2.17

•

We begin by proving the first point.
This first point is essentially an application of
Proposition

3.11
. Indeed, let
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell},\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
be the sequence satisfying the property stated in
Theorem

2.17
. By setting
\mathrm{P}^{\ell}:=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{\alpha^{\ell}},W,B,R^{\ell},\mu^{\ell}\right)^{-1}
where
R^{\ell}:=\delta_{(\overline{\mu}^{\ell}_{t},\aleph^{\ell}_{t})}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t
for each
\ell\geq 1
, by
Proposition

3.11
, we know that the sequence
(\mathrm{P}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
and each limit point
\mathrm{P}
of a convergent sub–sequence
\left(\mathrm{P}^{\ell_{k}}\right)_{k\geq 1}
belongs to
\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
associated to
(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})
that satisfies:
\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{\ell_{k}},\;R^{\ell_{k}}\right)=\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}(R)(\mathrm{d}r)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{\ell_{k}},\;\delta_{\phi^{n_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mu^{\ell_{k}}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;R^{\ell_{k}}\right)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu,\;\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;R\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}.

Let
\mathrm{Q}^{k}:={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu^{\ell_{k}},\;\delta_{\phi^{n_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\mu^{\ell_{k}}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;\xi^{\ell_{k}},\;R^{\ell_{k}}\right)
. It is straightforward that the sequence
(\mathrm{Q}^{k})_{k\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
. Let
\mathrm{Q}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\mu,\kappa,\xi,R\right)^{-1}
be the limit of a convergent sub–sequence. Let us keep the same notation for the sequence and the sub–sequence for simplification. By the convergence showed previously and the definition of the variables, we easily check that
\mu=H(R)
where
H
is a Borel map and
\kappa=\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)

\mathbb{P}
–a.e.. Consequently,

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}J^{\alpha^{\ell_{k}},\overline{\mu}^{\ell_{k}}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell_{k}})

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[U\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mu^{\ell_{k}}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{\mathrm{P}}\left(x,\phi^{\ell_{k}}(x)\right)\mu^{\ell_{k}}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)-\overline{g}_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu^{\ell_{k}},\xi^{\ell_{k}}\right)-\langle\overline{L}_{\mathrm{P}},R^{\ell_{k}}\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[U\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{\mathrm{P}}\left(x,i\right)\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)-\overline{g}_{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu,\xi\right)-\langle\overline{L}_{\mathrm{P}},R\rangle\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{M}\times{\rm E}}U\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Upsilon(x)H_{T}({\rm r})(\mathrm{d}x)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{\mathrm{P}}\left(x,i\right)\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)H_{T}({\rm r})(\mathrm{d}x)-\overline{g}_{\mathrm{P}}\left(H({\rm r}),\xi\right)-\langle\overline{L}_{\mathrm{P}},{\rm r}\rangle\right)\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}(R)(\mathrm{d}r)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{R,\mu}_{\mathrm{P}}(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})\big].

This is true for any sub–sequence of
(\mathrm{Q})_{k\geq 1}
, we can then deduce the convergence just proved for the all sequence.

By combining
Proposition

3.5
and
Proposition

3.7
, there exist a contract
\mathfrak{C}=(\phi,\xi,\aleph)
and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]\leq J_{A,\overline{\mu}}^{\mathfrak{C}}(\alpha)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{R,\mu}_{\mathrm{P}}(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})\big]\leq J^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}).

This is enough to conclude the proof of the first part of the Theorem.

•

This is a direct application of
Proposition

3.18
.

3.5.2
Proof of
Theorem

2.15

Since
R_{0}
is s.t.
\Xi
is non–empty, let
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell},\mathfrak{C}^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
be the sequence satisfying: for each
\ell\geq 1
,
\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}=(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})
belongs to
\Xi
,
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in\underline{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}]
and
V_{\mathrm{P}}\leq J^{\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell})+\frac{1}{2^{\ell}}
. By considering
\varepsilon_{\ell}=0
for each
\ell\geq 1
, we can apply
Theorem

2.17
and find a sub–sequence
(\ell_{j})_{j\geq 1}
, a contract
\mathfrak{C}^{\star}
and
(\alpha^{\star},\overline{\mu}^{\star})
s.t.
(\alpha^{\star},\overline{\mu}^{\star})\in\mbox{{\rm MFG}}[\mathfrak{C}^{\star}]
,

\lim_{j\to\infty}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell_{j}}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell_{j}}}({\alpha^{\ell_{j}}})\leq J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\star}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\star}}({\alpha^{\star}})~~~~~\mbox{\rm and}~~~~\lim_{j\to\infty}J^{\alpha^{\ell_{j}},\overline{\mu}^{\ell_{j}}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell_{j}})\leq J^{\alpha^{\star},\overline{\mu}^{\star}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\star}).

Consequently, we deduce that
V_{\mathrm{P}}\leq J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\star}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\star}}({\alpha^{\star}})
. Since
(\alpha^{\star},\overline{\mu}^{\star})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}^{\star}]
and
R_{0}\leq J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\star}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\star}}({\alpha^{\star}})
, in fact
\mathfrak{C}^{\star}\in\Xi
and
V^{\mathrm{P}}=J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\star}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\star}}({\alpha^{\star}})
. The shape of the control is deduced by
Proposition

3.5
and
Proposition

3.7
.

3.5.3
Proof of
Corollary

2.19

Notice that, for
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\underline{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
, by the second point of
Theorem

2.17
, there exist a sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t.
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\ell}=0,
and a sequence
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell},\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t. for each
\ell\geq 1
,
\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}=(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})
is a contract,
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in\mbox{{\rm MFG}}_{S}[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell}]
and
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})=J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}({\alpha}).
Therefore, by setting
\varepsilon_{0,\ell}:=\left|J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})-J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}(\alpha)\right|
,

J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})=J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}(\alpha)+J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})-J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}(\alpha)\geq R_{0}+J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})-J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}(\alpha)\geq R_{0}-\varepsilon_{0,\ell}.

We have
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in\underline{{\rm MFG}}_{S}[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell},\varepsilon_{0,\ell}]
. Since,
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\ell}=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\varepsilon_{0,\ell}=0
, we deduce that
\Xi_{S}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
is non–empty for each
\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}>0
.

Let
(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}},\alpha^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}},\overline{\mu}^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}})_{\ell\geq 1,\varepsilon>0,\varepsilon_{0}>0}
be a sequence s.t.
\mathfrak{C}^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}}\in\Xi[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
,
(\alpha^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}},\overline{\mu}^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}})\in\underline{\rm MFG}_{S}[\mathfrak{C},\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
and for each
\ell\geq 1
,
\varepsilon,\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle V^{S}_{\mathrm{P}}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]\leq J^{\alpha^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}},\overline{\mu}^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}})+\frac{1}{2^{\ell}}.

We apply the first point of
Theorem

2.17
to find a sub–sequence
(\ell_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j})_{j\geq 1}
, a contract
\mathfrak{C}
and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
s.t.
\lim_{j\to\infty}J^{\alpha^{\ell_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j}},\overline{\mu}^{\ell_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j}}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j}})\leq J^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C})
. It is straightforward that
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\underline{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
. This allows us to deduce that

\displaystyle\limsup_{(\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,0)}V^{S}_{\mathrm{P}}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]\leq V_{\mathrm{P}}.

Now, let
\mathfrak{C}
be a contract and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\underline{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
. Next, we apply the second point of
Theorem

2.17
(combined with the previous point) and find a sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t.
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\varepsilon_{\ell}=0,
and a sequence
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell},\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}
s.t. for each
\ell\geq 1
,
\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}=(\phi^{\ell},\xi^{\ell},\aleph^{\ell})
is a contract,
(\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell})\in\underline{{\rm MFG}}_{S}[\mathfrak{C}^{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell},\varepsilon_{0,\ell}]
and
\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J^{\mathfrak{C}^{\ell}}_{A,\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}(\alpha^{\ell})=J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}({\alpha})~~~~~\mbox{\rm and}~~~~\lim_{\ell\to\infty}J^{\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell})=J^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}).

For each
\delta,\delta_{0}>0
, there exists
\ell(\delta,\delta_{0})
s.t. for each
\ell\geq\ell(\delta,\delta_{0})
,
\varepsilon_{\ell}\leq\delta
and
\varepsilon_{0,\ell}\leq\delta_{0}
. Therefore, for each
\ell\geq\ell(\delta,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle J^{\alpha^{\ell},\overline{\mu}^{\ell}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{\ell})\leq V_{\mathrm{P}}^{S}[\delta,\delta_{0}].

Then,
J^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C})\leq V^{S}_{\mathrm{P}}[\delta,\delta_{0}]
for each
\delta,\delta_{0}>0
. This leads to
V_{\mathrm{P}}\leq\liminf_{(\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,0)}V^{S}_{\mathrm{P}}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
. By combining, all the results,

V_{\mathrm{P}}=\lim_{(\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,0)}V^{S}_{\mathrm{P}}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}].

We can conclude the proof since the second point follows immediately.

3.5.4
Proof of
Theorem

2.20

•

We start by proving the first point. This proof is quite close to the proof of
Theorem

2.17
. Let
(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n},\phi^{n},\xi^{n},\aleph^{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence given as in the Theorem. We set
\mathrm{P}^{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\circ\left(X^{i},W^{i},B,R^{n},\varphi^{n}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}]\right)^{-1}

\mbox{where}\;R^{n}:=\delta_{\left(\overline{\varphi}^{n}_{t}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}],\aleph^{n}(t,\mathbf{X}_{t})\right)}(\mathrm{d}m,\mathrm{d}e)\mathrm{d}t.
By
Proposition

3.9
, the sequence
(\mathrm{P}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
and any limit point
\mathrm{P}
of a convergent sub–sequence
\left(\mathrm{P}^{n_{k}}\right)_{k\geq 1}
is a MFG solution i.e. belongs to
\overline{{\cal P}}_{\rm mfg}
associated to
(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})
which verifies:
\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi^{n_{k}}(\mathbf{X}),\;R^{n_{k}}\right)=\mathfrak{Z}(r)(\mathrm{d}e){\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(R\right)(\mathrm{d}r)
and

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\varphi^{n_{k}}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n_{k}}],\;\delta_{\phi^{n_{k}}(x)}(\mathrm{d}i)\varphi^{n_{k}}_{T}[\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n_{k}}](\mathrm{d}x),\;R^{n_{k}}\right)={\cal L}^{\mathrm{P}}\left(\mu,\;\Phi(x)(\mathrm{d}i)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;R\right)\;\mbox{in}\;{\cal W}_{p}.

Similarly to the proof of
Theorem

2.17
, we combine
Proposition

3.5
and
Proposition

3.7
and find a contract
\mathfrak{C}=(\phi,\xi,\aleph)
and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
satisfying
\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{\Phi,\mathfrak{Z}}_{A}(\Gamma^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},R,\mu)\big]\leq J_{A,\overline{\mu}}^{\mathfrak{C}}(\alpha)\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\mathbb{E}^{{\mathrm{P}}}\big[{\rm J}^{R,\mu}_{\mathrm{P}}(\Phi,\mathfrak{Z})\big]\leq J^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}).

We can then conclude the proof of the first part of the Theorem.

•

This second point is just an application of
Proposition

3.18
combined with
Proposition

3.19
.

3.5.5
Proof of
Corollary

2.22

The proof of this Corollary follows the same idea as the proof of
Corollary

2.19
. By mimicking the proof of
Corollary

2.19
combined with the second part of
Theorem

2.20
, we see that, for each
\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}>0
, there exists
\overline{n}
s.t. for each
n\geq\overline{n}
,
\Xi^{\rm dist}_{n}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
is non–empty.

Let
(\mathfrak{C}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}},\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}})_{n\geq 1,\varepsilon>0,\varepsilon_{0}>0}
be a sequence s.t.
\mathfrak{C}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}}\in\Xi_{n}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
,
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}}\in\underline{\rm NE}[\mathfrak{C}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}},\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]
and for
n\geq 1

V_{n,\mathrm{P}}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]\leq J^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}}}_{n,\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{n,\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}})+\frac{1}{2^{n}}.

By
Theorem

2.20
, there exist a sub–sequence
(n_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j})_{j\geq 1}
, a contract
\mathfrak{C}
and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
satisfying

\lim_{j\to\infty}J^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j}}}_{n_{j},\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}^{n_{j},\varepsilon_{j},\varepsilon_{0,j}})\leq J^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}_{\mathrm{P}}(\mathfrak{C}).

It is straightforward that
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\underline{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
. This allows us to see that

\limsup_{(\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0})\to(0,0)}\limsup_{n\to\infty}V_{n,\mathrm{P}}[\varepsilon,\varepsilon_{0}]\leq V_{\mathrm{P}}.

Next, let
\mathfrak{C}
be an admissible contract and
(\alpha,\overline{\mu})\in\underline{\rm MFG}[\mathfrak{C}]
, by
Theorem

2.20
, there exist a sequence of non–negative numbers
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\geq 1}
s.t.
\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0,
and a sequence
(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n},\mathfrak{C}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
with
\mathfrak{C}^{n}
a
distributed
contract and
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}\in{\rm NE}^{\rm dist}[\mathfrak{C}^{n},\varepsilon_{n}]
for each
n\geq 1
, and we have
\lim_{n\to\infty}J_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}}}(\mathfrak{C}^{n})=J_{\mathrm{P}}^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}(\mathfrak{C})
. We set

{\varepsilon}_{0,n}:=\left|J^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}_{n,i}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n})-J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}(\alpha)\right|=\left|J^{\mathfrak{C}^{n}}_{n,1}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n})-J^{\mathfrak{C}}_{A,\overline{\mu}}(\alpha)\right|.

We have
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}\in\underline{\rm NE}^{\rm dist}[\mathfrak{C}^{n},\varepsilon_{n},{\varepsilon}_{0,n}]
. Since
\lim_{n\to\infty}{\varepsilon}_{0,n}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0
, let
\delta,\delta_{0}>0
, there exists
n(\delta,\delta_{0})\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
s.t. for any
n\geq n(\delta,\delta_{0})
, we have
\varepsilon_{n}\leq\delta
and
\varepsilon_{0,n}\leq\delta_{0}
. Consequently, for any
n\geq n(\delta,\delta_{0})
,

\displaystyle J_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{n}}}(\mathfrak{C}^{n})\leq V_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{\rm dist}[\delta,\delta_{0}].

Then
J_{\mathrm{P}}^{\alpha,\overline{\mu}}(\mathfrak{C})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}V_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{\rm dist}[\delta,\delta_{0}]
, for any
\delta,\delta_{0}>0
. This leads to
V_{\mathrm{P}}\leq\liminf_{(\delta,\delta_{0})\to(0,0)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}V_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{\rm dist}[\delta,\delta_{0}].

By summarizing, we find

V_{\mathrm{P}}\leq\liminf_{(\delta,\delta_{0})\to(0,0)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}V_{n,\mathrm{P}}^{\rm dist}[\delta,\delta_{0}]\leq\limsup_{(\delta,\delta_{0})\to(0,0)}\limsup_{n\to\infty}V^{\rm dist}_{n,\mathrm{P}}[\delta,\delta_{0}]\leq V_{\mathrm{P}}

and

V_{\mathrm{P}}\leq\liminf_{(\delta,\delta_{0})\to(0,0)}\liminf_{n\to\infty}V_{n,\mathrm{P}}[\delta,\delta_{0}]\leq\limsup_{(\delta,\delta_{0})\to(0,0)}\limsup_{n\to\infty}V_{n,\mathrm{P}}[\delta,\delta_{0}]\leq V_{\mathrm{P}}.

We deduce the proof of the Corollary.

Appendix A
Technical results

Let
({\cal S},\Delta)
be a Polish space and
v
be a progressively Borel map s.t.
[0,T]\times\mathbb{M}({\cal S})\times\mathbb{R}\ni(t,z,x)\to v(t,z,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is Lipschitz in
x
uniformly in
(t,z)
and
m{\rm I}_{d}\leq vv^{\top}\leq M{\rm I}_{d}
for some
0<m\leq M
.
On the probability space
\left(\Omega,\mathbb{H},\mathbb{P}\right)
, let
(b_{t},Z_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
be an
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times{\cal P}({\cal S})
–valued
\mathbb{H}
–predictable process with
(Z_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
independent of
(W,\iota)
. The process
Z=(Z_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
will be consider as an element of
\mathbb{M}({\cal S})
. We consider
X
the process satisfying:
X_{0}:=\iota
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}=b_{t}\mathrm{d}t+v(t,Z,X_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}.

We set
\mu=(\mu_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
by
\mu_{t}={\cal L}(X_{t}|Z)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Let
\widehat{X}
be the process satisfying:
\widehat{X}_{0}=\iota
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\widehat{X}_{t}=\widehat{b}(t,\widehat{X}_{t},\mu,Z)\mathrm{d}t+v(t,Z,\widehat{X}_{t})\mathrm{d}W_{t}\;\;\mbox{where}\;\;\widehat{b}(t,x,\pi,z):=\mathbb{E}\Big[b_{t}\Big|X_{t}=x,\mu_{t\wedge\cdot}=\pi(t\wedge\cdot),Z_{t\wedge\cdot}=z(t\wedge\cdot)\Big].

(A.1)

Lemma A.1
.

The process
\widehat{X}
verifies:

\displaystyle{\cal L}(X_{t}|Z)={\cal L}(\widehat{X}_{t}|Z)={\cal L}(\widehat{X}_{t}|Z_{t\wedge\cdot},\mu_{t\wedge\cdot}),\;\;\mbox{for all}\;t\in[0,T],\;\;\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Proof.

For simplification we only treat the case
d=1
. Since
Z
is independent of
(W,\iota)
,
by applying Itô Formula and taking the conditional expectation w.r.t.
\sigma\{Z\}
, we check that: for any twice differentiable bounded map
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, and
s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\langle\varphi,\mu_{t}\rangle-\langle\varphi,\mu_{s}\rangle-\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v^{2}(t,Z,x)\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)\mu_{r}(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}r=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{\prime}(x)\mathbb{E}[b_{r}|X_{r}=x,Z]\mu_{r}(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}r.

Therefore, there exists a progressively Borel measurable map
H^{\varphi}:[0,T]\times C([0,T];{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}))\times\mathbb{M}({\cal S})\to\mathbb{R}
s.t.

H^{\varphi}(t,\mu,Z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{\prime}(x)\mathbb{E}[b_{t}|X_{t}=x,Z]\mu_{t}(\mathrm{d}x),\;\;\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\mbox{--a.e.}

Consequently by conditioning w.r.t.
\sigma\{Z_{t\wedge\cdot},\mu_{t\wedge\cdot}\}
, we obtain that
H^{\varphi}(t,\mu,Z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi^{\prime}(x)\mathbb{E}[b_{t}|X_{t}=x,\mu_{t\wedge\cdot},Z_{t\wedge\cdot}]\mu_{t}(\mathrm{d}x)
,
\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
–a.e. This is true for any
\varphi
. This is enough to see that the process
\widehat{X}
defined in (
A.1
) satisfies the statement of the Lemma. We can conclude.
∎

Now, we consider that the random variable
Z
is independent of
(W^{i},\iota^{i})_{i\geq 1}
s.t.
\mathbb{E}[\Delta(Z,z_{0})^{p^{\prime}}]<\infty
for some
z_{0}\in{\cal S}
. For each
i\geq 1
,
X^{i,n}
is the
\mathbb{R}^{d}
–valued
\mathbb{F}
–adapted process satisfying:
X^{i,n}_{0}=\iota^{i}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X^{i,n}_{t}=b^{i,n}_{t}\mathrm{d}t+v(t,Z,X^{i,n}_{t})\mathrm{d}W^{i}_{t}

where for each
n\geq 1
,
(b^{i,n})_{1\leq i\leq n}
is a sequence of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
–valued
\mathbb{F}
–predictable processes, and the following condition is satisfies:

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}|b^{i,n}_{t}|^{p^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

(A.2)

Let
(\phi^{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of Borel map verifying
\phi^{n}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to{\cal V}
where
{\cal V}
is some Polish space and

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}v){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x={\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}v){\rm G}(x)\mathrm{d}x\;\mbox{for the weak convergence}

where
{\rm R}
is a kernel i.e.
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto{\rm R}(x)\in{\cal P}({\cal V})
is a Borel map and
{\rm G}
is the density
{\rm G}(x):=\frac{1}{1+|x|^{p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{p}}\mathrm{d}y\right)^{-1}
. Recall that
1<p<p^{\prime}
. We define the sequence
(\mathrm{P}^{n})_{n\geq 1}\subset{\cal P}\left({\cal P}({\cal V}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\times{\cal C}_{\cal W}\times{\cal S}\right)
by

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{n}=\mathbb{P}\circ\left(\delta_{\phi^{n}(x)}(\mathrm{d}v)\varphi^{n}_{T}(\mathrm{d}x),\;\;\varphi^{n},\;Z\right)^{-1}\;\;\mbox{where}\;\;\varphi^{n}:=(\varphi^{n}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}:=\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{X^{i}_{t}}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)_{t\in[0,T]}.

Proposition A.2
.

The sequence
(\mathrm{P}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is relatively compact in
{\cal W}_{p}
. In addition, each limit point
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}\left(\kappa,\mu,Z\right)^{-1}
verifies:
\mathbb{P}
–a.e.

\displaystyle{\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}v)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)=\kappa(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}x).

Proof.

By using condition (
A.2
) and
\mathbb{E}[\Delta(Z,z_{0})^{p^{\prime}}]<\infty
, the relative compactness of
(\mathrm{P}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
follows from classical techniques. We take
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}\left(\kappa,\mu,Z\right)^{-1}
the limit of a sub–sequence. For simplicity, we use the same notation for the sequence and its sub–sequence. Let us show the second point. For this purpose, we first show that: for any bounded continuous
(F^{q})_{1\leq q\leq Q}
and
(G^{k})_{1\leq k\leq K}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{K}\langle G^{k},\mu_{T}\rangle\prod_{q=1}^{Q}\int_{{\cal V}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}F^{q}(v,x)\kappa(\mathrm{d}v,\mathrm{d}x)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{K}\langle G^{k},\mu_{T}\rangle\prod_{q=1}^{Q}\int_{{\cal V}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}F^{q}(v,x){\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}v)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right].

(A.3)

We only prove this result for
Q=K=2
. The generalization is straightforward from this case. Notice that, for each
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{2}\langle G^{k},\varphi^{n}_{T}\rangle\prod_{q=1}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}F^{q}\left(\phi^{n}(X^{i,n}_{T}),X^{i,n}_{T}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})F^{1}(\phi^{n}(x^{3}),x^{3})F^{2}(\phi^{n}(x^{4}),x^{4}){\cal L}\left(X^{i_{1}}_{T},X^{i_{2}}_{T},X^{i_{3}}_{T},X^{i_{4}}_{T}|Z\right)(\mathrm{d}x^{1},\mathrm{d}x^{2},\mathrm{d}x^{3},\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right].

When the indexes
\boldsymbol{i}:=(i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4})
are all distinct, since
m{\rm I}_{d\times d}\leq vv^{\top}\leq M{\rm I}_{d\times d}
and
Z
is independent of
(W^{i},\iota^{i})_{i\geq 1}
, for any
z\in{\cal S}
, the probability
{\cal L}\left(X^{i_{1}}_{T},X^{i_{2}}_{T},X^{i_{3}}_{T},X^{i_{4}}_{T}|Z=z\right)
has a density
f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(z)(T,\mathbf{x})
w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure, we noted
\mathbf{x}:=(x^{1},x^{2},x^{3},x^{4})
. We can choose
f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}
s.t.
[0,T]\times{\cal S}\times\mathbb{R}^{4}\ni(t,z,\mathbf{x})\mapsto f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(z)(t,\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}
is Borel measurable. By
[
15
, Proposition A.1.]
(see also
[
1
, Theorem 4]
and
[
3
, Theorem 6.2.7]
), for each compact
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^{4}
, there exists
\delta\in(0,1)
(independent of
z
) s.t.

\displaystyle\sup_{n\geq 1}\;\sup_{z\in{\cal S}}\sup_{\boldsymbol{i}\mbox{ distinct}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\neq\mathbf{x}^{\prime},(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in\Gamma\times\Gamma}\frac{|f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(z)(T,\mathbf{x})-f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(z)(T,\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|}{|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\delta}}<\infty.

We can then conclude, for any bounded map
h
, the sequence of maps

\left(\mathbb{R}^{4}\ni\mathbf{x}\mapsto\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\mbox{ distinct}}\mathbb{E}\left[h(Z)f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(Z)(T,\mathbf{x})\right]\in\mathbb{R}\right)_{n\geq 1}

is relatively compact in the set of continuous functions for the locally uniform topology. Using the weak convergence, we can show that the whole sequence converges and that the limit is
P(\mathbf{x})
that verifies: for any continuous map
C

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}C(\mathbf{x})P(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}C(\mathbf{x})h(Z)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{1})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{2})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{3})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right].

Consequently, we can see that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Gamma}\left|\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\mbox{ distinct}}\mathbb{E}\left[h(Z)f^{n}(Z)(T,\mathbf{x})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[h(Z)f(Z)(T,\mathbf{x})\right]\right|\;\mbox{for any compact }\Gamma\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}

(A.4)

\mbox{where}\;f(Z)(T,\mathbf{x})\;\mbox{is the density of}\;\mathbb{E}\left[\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{1})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{2})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{3})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{4})|Z\right]\in{\cal P}(\mathbb{R}^{4})
. We can then rewrite

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\;\mbox{distincts}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})F^{1}(\phi^{n}(x^{3}),x^{3})F^{2}(\phi^{n}(x^{4}),x^{4}){\cal L}\left(X^{i_{1}}_{T},X^{i_{2}}_{T},X^{i_{3}}_{T},X^{i_{4}}_{T}|Z\right)(\mathrm{d}x^{1},\mathrm{d}x^{2},\mathrm{d}x^{3},\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2}){\rm G}^{-1}(x^{3}){\rm G}^{-1}(x^{4})\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\;\mbox{distinct}}\mathbb{E}\left[f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(Z)(T,\mathbf{x}))\right]\mathrm{d}x^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{2}\prod_{q=3}^{4}\left(\int_{A}F^{q-2}(a,x^{q})\delta_{\phi^{n}(x^{q})}(\mathrm{d}a){\rm G}(x^{q})\mathrm{d}x^{q}\right).

By combining the locally uniform convergence of the sequence in (
A.4
), the weak convergence and the fact that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|X^{i,n}_{t}|^{p^{\prime}}\right]<\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2}){\rm G}^{-1}(x^{3}){\rm G}^{-1}(x^{4})\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\;\mbox{distinct}}\mathbb{E}\left[f^{n}_{\boldsymbol{i}}(Z)(T,\mathbf{x}))\right]\mathrm{d}x^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{2}\prod_{q=3}^{4}\int_{A}F^{q-2}(a,x^{q})\delta_{\phi^{n}(x^{q})}(\mathrm{d}a){\rm G}(x^{q})\mathrm{d}x^{q}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2}){\rm G}^{-1}(x^{3}){\rm G}^{-1}(x^{4})\mathbb{E}\left[f(Z)(T,\mathbf{x}))\right]\mathrm{d}x^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{2}\prod_{q=3}^{4}\int_{A}F^{q-2}(a,x^{q}){\rm R}(x^{q})(\mathrm{d}a){\rm G}(x^{q})\mathrm{d}x^{q}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})\mathbb{E}\left[f(Z)(T,\mathbf{x}))\right]\mathrm{d}x^{1}\mathrm{d}x^{2}\prod_{q=3}^{4}\int_{A}F^{q-2}(a,x^{q}){\rm R}(x^{q})(\mathrm{d}a)\mathrm{d}x^{q}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})\int_{A}F^{3}(a,x^{3}){\rm R}(x^{3})(\mathrm{d}a)\int_{A}F^{4}(a,x^{4}){\rm R}(x^{4})(\mathrm{d}a)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{1})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{2})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{3})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right].

By combining our results and the fact that
(G^{1},G^{2},F^{1},F^{2})
are bounded, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{2}\langle G^{k},\mu_{T}\rangle\prod_{q=1}^{2}\int_{\mathrm{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}F^{q}(a,x)\kappa(\mathrm{d}a,\mathrm{d}x)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{2}\langle G^{k},\varphi^{n}_{T}\rangle\prod_{q=1}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}F^{q}\left(\phi^{n}(X^{i,n}_{T}),X^{i,n}_{T}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})F^{1}(\phi^{n}(x^{3}),x^{3})F^{2}(\phi^{n}(x^{4}),x^{4}){\cal L}\left(X^{i_{1}}_{T},X^{i_{2}}_{T},X^{i_{3}}_{T},X^{i_{4}}_{T}|Z\right)(\mathrm{d}x^{1},\mathrm{d}x^{2},\mathrm{d}x^{3},\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{\boldsymbol{i}\;\mbox{distinct}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})F^{1}(\phi^{n}(x^{3}),x^{3})F^{2}(\phi^{n}(x^{4}),x^{4}){\cal L}\left(X^{i_{1}}_{T},X^{i_{2}}_{T},X^{i_{3}}_{T},X^{i_{4}}_{T}|Z\right)(\mathrm{d}x^{1},\mathrm{d}x^{2},\mathrm{d}x^{3},\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4}}G^{1}(x^{1})G^{2}(x^{2})\int_{A}F^{3}(a,x^{3}){\rm R}(x^{3})(\mathrm{d}a)\int_{A}F^{4}(a,x^{4}){\rm R}(x^{4})(\mathrm{d}a)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{1})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{2})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{3})\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x^{4})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{2}\langle G^{k},\mu_{T}\rangle\prod_{q=1}^{2}\int_{\mathrm{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}F^{q}(a,x){\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}a)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right].

We conclude the result in (
A.3
). Since (
A.3
) is true for any
(F^{q})_{1\leq q\leq Q}
and
(G^{k})_{1\leq k\leq K}
, by
[
17
, Proposition A.3.]
, we can say that
{\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}(\mu_{T},\kappa)={\cal L}^{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu_{T},{\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}v)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right)
. This leads to
\kappa={\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}v)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)
,
\mathbb{P}
–a.e. Indeed, for a continuous bounded map
h
, by the equality in distribution we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left|\langle h,\kappa\rangle-\int_{{\cal V}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}h(v,x){\rm R}(x)(\mathrm{d}v)\mu_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right|\right]=0.

∎

Let us consider a closed convex set
(U,\Delta^{U})
, a Polish space
({\cal Z},\Delta^{{\cal Z}})
and a Borel measurable map
(f,h):{\cal Z}\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}
. We assume that for any
\eta\in{\cal Z}
, the set

\left\{\left(f(\eta,u),z\right):\;z\leq h(\eta,u),\;\;u\in U\right\}

is a closed convex set.

Proposition A.3
.

There exists a universally measurable map
{\cal U}:{\cal Z}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\to U
s.t. for any
q\in{\cal P}(U)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})=f\left(\eta,\;{\cal U}\left(\eta,\;\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}),\;\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\right)\right)

and

\displaystyle\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\leq h\left(\eta,\;{\cal U}\left(\eta,\;\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}),\;\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\right)\right).

Proof.

We apply the measurable selection Theorem of
[
20
, Theorem 2.20.]
. Indeed, let us first observe that the set
A:=\{(\eta,y,z,u):\;f(\eta,u)=y,\;z\leq h(\eta,u)\}
is a Borel set. Then, by
[
20
, Theorem 2.20.]
, there exists a universally measurable map
{\cal U}:{\cal Z}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\to U
s.t.

\displaystyle\left\{\left(\eta,y,z,{\cal U}(\eta,y,z)\right)\right\}\subset A

and

\displaystyle\Pi_{{\cal Z}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}}(A):=\left\{(\eta,y,z):\;\;\exists u\in U\;\mbox{s.t.}\;f(\eta,u)=y,\;z\leq h(\eta,u)\right\}=\left\{(\eta,y,z):\;\;{\cal U}(\eta,y,z)\in U\right\}.

Given the assumption verified by
(f,h)
, for any
q\in{\cal P}(U)
,
\left(\eta,\;\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}),\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\right)
belongs to
\Pi_{{\cal Z}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}}(A)
. This means that

\displaystyle\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})=f\left(\eta,\;{\cal U}\left(\eta,\;\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}),\;\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\right)\right)

and

\displaystyle\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\leq h\left(\eta,\;{\cal U}\left(\eta,\;\int_{U}f(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}),\;\int_{U}h(\eta,u^{\prime})q(\mathrm{d}u^{\prime})\right)\right).

∎
